NEW SECTION
WAC 246-453-021   Procedures for final determination of charity 
care eligibility.   Each hospital shall implement the following re-
quirements for making a final determination of charity care eligibili-
ty, except that a hospital may modify its process to be less burden-
some for a responsible party:
(1) A hospital may require a responsible party who has been ini-
tially determined eligible for charity care under WAC 246-453-020 
(7)(c) to complete the final determination process. A hospital, in its 
discretion, may deem an initial determination that a responsible party 
is eligible for charity care to be a final determination, though the 
hospital shall permit a responsible party to complete the final deter-
mination process if the responsible party so chooses.
(2) A hospital shall permit a responsible party who is initially 
determined ineligible for charity care under WAC 246-453-020 (8) to 
complete the final determination process if the responsible party so 
chooses.
(3) A responsible party who was initially determined ineligible 
for the patient's or guarantor's failure to make reasonable efforts to 
cooperate with the hospital's efforts to assist the patient or guaran-
Washington State Register, Issue 26-16 WSR 26-16-047
Certified on 8/13/2026 [ 29 ] WSR Issue 26-16 - Proposed

tor in applying for retroactive health care coverage under WAC 
246-453-020(2) must be provided another opportunity during the final 
determination process to cooperate, and the hospital shall assist them 
in applying for retroactive health coverage and make a final determi-
nation in accordance with this section.
(4) A hospital may require a responsible party to submit a chari-
ty care application, including any requested documentation as permit-
ted under WAC 246-453-030, by a reasonable deadline provided in MM/DD/
YYYY format that takes into account the responsible party's personal 
circumstances and the difficulty of securing and submitting the docu-
mentation requested by the hospital, though under no circumstances may 
the deadline be less than 30 days after the hospital sent the notice 
required under WAC 246-453-020 (7)(c) or (8), as applicable.
(a) A hospital may use the charity care application template is-
sued by the department or an application that is equivalent to the de-
partment's template and does not request additional information or 
documentation beyond what is allowable in this chapter and chapter 
70.170 RCW.
(b) The hospital shall assist the responsible party with complet-
ing the charity care application as needed.
(c) The hospital may require the responsible party to attest to 
the accuracy of the information provided.
(5) In making a final determination of charity care eligibility, 
the hospital shall rely on the information the responsible party pro-
vides on the charity care application and in any submitted documenta-
tion. The hospital shall determine within which, if any, of the appli-
cable income ranges specified in RCW 70.170.060 (5) the responsible 
party's annual income, adjusted for family size, falls. The hospital 
shall determine the patient responsibility portion of the hospital 
charges by applying to the hospital charges the applicable percentage 
discount under RCW 70.170.060 (5) or the hospital's policy, whichever 
is higher, making, in the hospital's discretion, any permitted reduc-
tion to the amount of the discount for amounts reasonably related to 
assets. If the hospital is permitted to and chooses to consider the 
responsible party's assets to reduce the discount under RCW 
70.170.060(5), the hospital may require the responsible party to sub-
mit asset information, as permitted under WAC 246-453-030, by a rea-
sonable deadline provided in MM/DD/YYYY format that takes into account 
the responsible party's personal circumstances and the difficulty of 
securing and submitting the documentation requested by the hospital, 
though under no circumstances may the deadline be less than 30 days 
after the hospital sent the request.
(6) The hospital shall provide a responsible party notice of the 
final determination within 30 days of submission of the charity care 
application and supporting documentation, which must include the in-
formation listed in this subsection. If any of the information is not 
available at the time when notice is required to be provided, the hos-
pital shall provide the responsible party an updated notice within 15 
days of when the information becomes available.
(a) A statement indicating whether the responsible party has been 
granted or denied charity care;
(b) The responsible party's income, as determined by the hospi-
tal, presented as a total and broken down into the categories included 
in the definition of income in WAC 246-453-010;
(c) The composition of the responsible party's family, as deter-
mined by the hospital;
(d) The amount of the hospital charges;
Washington State Register, Issue 26-16 WSR 26-16-047
Certified on 8/13/2026 [ 30 ] WSR Issue 26-16 - Proposed

(e) The amount of the patient responsibility portion of the hos-
pital charges;
(f) If applicable, the percentage discount applied, the amount of 
the discount, and the amount of any reduction made to the discount for 
amounts reasonably related to assets;
(g) The dates of past services and prospective services, if any, 
to which the discounts will be applied; and
(h) Information about the opportunity to appeal under WAC 
246-453-025.
(7) If a responsible party expressly declines to complete the fi-
nal determination process, fails to meet an applicable deadline, or 
fails to make reasonable efforts to cooperate with the hospital's ef-
forts to complete the final determination process, the responsible 
party is finally determined ineligible for charity care. The hospital 
shall provide the responsible party notice of the final determination, 
which must include:
(a) A statement that the responsible party has been finally de-
termined ineligible for charity care with an explanation of the basis 
for the determination; and
(b) Information about the opportunity to appeal under WAC 
246-453-025.
(8) Provided, that the hospital has complied with all applicable 
requirements, the hospital may initiate collection efforts directed at 
a responsible party who:
(a) Expressly declines to complete the final determination proc-
ess, fails to meet an applicable deadline, or fails to make reasonable 
efforts to cooperate with the hospital's efforts to complete the final 
determination process, 30 days after the hospital sent the required 
notice;
(b) Is finally determined ineligible for charity care, 30 days 
after the hospital sent the required notice; or
(c) Is finally determined to be eligible for charity care, but 
responsible for a portion of the hospital charges, though such collec-
tion efforts may be directed only at the patient responsibility por-
tion of the hospital charges and may commence only after 30 days after 
the date the hospital sent the required notice.
(9) A hospital may not refer the account of any responsible party 
who is entitled to appeal under WAC 246-453-025 to an external collec-
tion agency until the appeal period expires and any submitted appeal 
is decided.
(10) A hospital shall promptly cease any collection efforts di-
rected at a responsible party who is finally determined eligible for 
charity care for the full amount of the patient responsibility portion 
of their hospital charges.
(11) A hospital shall reimburse within 30 days a responsible par-
ty who is finally determined eligible for charity care for any funds 
collected in excess of the patient responsibility portion of their 
hospital charges.
NEW SECTION
WAC 246-453-025   Appeal procedures.   (1) Each hospital shall pro-
vide every responsible party who receives a final determination of 
charity care eligibility that includes any amount of patient responsi-
bility, an opportunity to appeal under this section. The appeal proc-
Washington State Register, Issue 26-16 WSR 26-16-047
Certified on 8/13/2026 [ 31 ] WSR Issue 26-16 - Proposed

ess must enable the responsible party to correct any deficiencies in 
documentation and provide for review of the final determination by the 
hospital's chief financial officer or equivalent or a delegate.
(2) The hospital shall give the responsible party at least 30 
days after receiving a notice of final determination to submit an ap-
peal. Upon request of a responsible party, a hospital shall provide a 
reasonable extension of the appeal deadline. A hospital's appeal pro-
cedure must permit a responsible party to make submissions to the hos-
pital by all reasonable means, including by submitting documents and 
information in hard copy in person and by mail and digitally by email 
and by a website accessible by smartphone and personal computer.
(3) As required under WAC 246-453-021, a hospital shall provide 
information about the opportunity to appeal under this section. Such 
notice must include the following or a substantially similar state-
ment: "If you disagree with the final determination of charity care 
eligibility, you may appeal by submitting additional documentation to 
correct deficiencies within your application or request review of the 
denial. Please submit your request and any additional documentation to 
our financial assistance office [insert relevant information]."
(4) A hospital shall provide a responsible party notice of its 
appeal decision within 30 days of submission of the appeal. The notice 
must include a detailed explanation of the basis for the decision and 
the information listed in WAC 246-453-021. An appeal may not result in 
an increase in the patient responsibility portion of the hospital 
charges.
(5) A hospital shall provide the department a copy of any appeal 
decision that does not grant the responsible party charity care for 
the full amount of the patient responsibility portion of their hospi-
tal charges and the documentation upon which the decision was based 
within 30 days of submission of the appeal.

the copy filed by the agency and appear in the Register pursuant to the requirements of RCW 34.08.040.
AMENDATORY SECTION  (Amending WSR 91-05-048, filed 2/14/91, effective 
3/17/91)
WAC 246-453-030   Data requirements for the identification of in-
digent persons.   (((1) For the purpose of reaching an initial determi-
nation of sponsorship status, hospitals shall rely upon information 
provided orally by the responsible party. The hospital may require the 
responsible party to sign a statement attesting to the accuracy of the 
information provided to the hospital for purposes of the initial de-
termination of sponsorship status.
(2) Any one of the following documents shall be considered suffi-
cient evidence upon which to base the final determination of charity 
care sponsorship status, when the income information is annualized as 
may be appropriate:
(a) A "W-2" withholding statement;
(b) Pay stubs;
(c) An income tax return from the most recently filed calendar 
year;
(d) Forms approving or denying eligibility for medicaid and/or 
state-funded medical assistance;
(e) Forms approving or denying unemployment compensation; or
(f) Written statements from employers or welfare agencies.
Washington State Register, Issue 26-16 WSR 26-16-047
Certified on 8/13/2026 [ 32 ] WSR Issue 26-16 - Proposed

(3) In the event that the responsible party's identification as 
an indigent person is obvious to hospital personnel, and the hospital 
personnel are able to establish the position of the income level with-
in the broad criteria described in WAC 246-453-040 or within income 
ranges included in the hospital's sliding fee schedule, the hospital 
is not obligated to establish the exact income level or to request the 
aforementioned documentation from the responsible party, unless the 
responsible party requests further review.
(4) In the event that the responsible party is not able to pro-
vide any of the documentation described above, the hospital shall rely 
upon written and signed statements from the responsible party for mak-
ing a final determination of eligibility for classification as an in-
digent person.
(5) Information requests, from the hospital to the responsible 
party, for the verification of income and family size shall be limited 
to that which is reasonably necessary and readily available to sub-
stantiate the responsible party's qualification for charity sponsor-
ship, and may not be used to discourage applications for such sponsor-
ship. Only those facts relevant to eligibility may be verified, and 
duplicate forms of verification shall not be demanded. )) (1) A hospi-
tal shall base charity care eligibility determinations made under this 
chapter on the responsible party's annual family income as follows:
(a) If the charity care application is made within two years of 
the time of service and the responsible party has been making good 
faith efforts towards payment of health care services provided, which 
include the responsible party making reasonable efforts to reach an 
agreement with the hospital regarding payment and to make payments in 
accordance with such agreement, the hospital shall use the responsible 
party's income as of the time of application; or
(b) If subsection (a) of this section does not apply, the hospi-
tal shall use the responsible party's income as of the time the health 
care services were provided. The hospital, in its discretion, may use 
the responsible party's income as of the time of application, if low-
er.
(2) A hospital may consider only the categories of income inclu-
ded in the definition of income in WAC 246-453-010 and request docu-
mentation of income only as permitted in this chapter and chapter 
70.170 RCW. A hospital may consider and request documentation of as-
sets only as permitted in this chapter and chapter 70.170 RCW.
(3) A hospital may request only such documentation as is reasona-
bly necessary to establish with reasonable accuracy the responsible 
party's income and assets at the applicable time under subsection (1) 
of this section. A hospital shall not make requests that place an un-
reasonable burden on the responsible party or discourage requests for 
charity care, and a hospital shall not request documentation that is 
not readily available, is unlikely to show the responsible party's in-
come or assets at the applicable time under subsection (1) of this 
section, is irrelevant, or is duplicative. A hospital shall not re-
quest the responsible party's Social Security number for purposes of 
making charity care determinations.
(4) A hospital shall accept a representation from a responsible 
party that they do not have income in a category listed in WAC 
246-453-010 or assets in a category about which the hospital has re-
quested information. A hospital shall accept any reasonably reliable 
form of documentation provided by a responsible party even if differ-
ent from the form of documentation requested by the hospital. A hospi-
tal shall accept a representation from a responsible party that they 
Washington State Register, Issue 26-16 WSR 26-16-047
Certified on 8/13/2026 [ 33 ] WSR Issue 26-16 - Proposed

do not have certain requested documentation and rely on the responsi-
ble party's representation as to the amount of such income or value of 
such asset.
(5) If a responsible party chooses to submit documentation that 
the hospital did not request, the hospital shall give due regard to 
such documentation in determining the responsible party's income and 
assets. The hospital shall provide the responsible party the opportu-
nity to submit explanatory information about the responsible party's 
income and assets including, but not limited to, information about any 
recent changes that would make any requested documentation an inaccu-
rate source of information, and the hospital shall give due regard to 
such information in determining the responsible party's income.

WAC 246-453-040   Uniform criteria for the identification of indi-
gent persons.   For the purpose of identifying indigent persons, all 
hospitals shall use the following criteria:
(1) ((All responsible parties with family income equal to or be-
low one hundred percent of the federal poverty standard, adjusted for 
family size, shall be determined to be indigent persons qualifying for 
charity sponsorship for the full amount of hospital charges related to 
appropriate hospital-based medical services that are not covered by 
private or public third-party sponsorship;
(2) All responsible parties with family income between one hun-
dred one and two hundred percent of the federal poverty standard, ad-
justed for family size, shall be determined to be indigent persons 
qualifying for discounts from charges related to appropriate hospital-
based medical services in accordance with the hospital's sliding fee 
schedule and policies regarding individual financial circumstances;
(3) Hospitals may classify any individual responsible party whose 
income exceeds two hundred percent of the federal poverty standard, 
adjusted for family size, as an indigent person eligible for a dis-
count from charges based upon that responsible party's individual fi-
nancial circumstances. )) At a minimum, a hospital owned or operated by 
a health system that owns or operates three or more acute hospitals 
licensed under chapter 70.41 RCW, an acute care hospital with over 300 
licensed beds located in the most populous county in Washington, or an 
acute care hospital with over 200 licensed beds located in a county 
with at least 450,000 residents and located on Washington's southern 
border shall grant charity care per the following guidelines:
(a) All patients and their guarantors whose income is not more 
than 300 percent of the federal poverty level, adjusted for family 
size, shall be deemed charity care patients for the full amount of the 
patient responsibility portion of their hospital charges;
(b) All patients and their guarantors whose income is between 301 
and 350 percent of the federal poverty level, adjusted for family 
size, shall be entitled to a 75 percent discount for the full amount 
of the patient responsibility portion of their hospital charges, which 
may be reduced by amounts reasonably related to assets considered pur-
suant to subsection (3) of this section;
(c) All patients and their guarantors whose income is between 351 
and 400 percent of the federal poverty level, adjusted for family 
size, shall be entitled to a 50 percent discount for the full amount 
Washington State Register, Issue 26-16 WSR 26-16-047
Certified on 8/13/2026 [ 34 ] WSR Issue 26-16 - Proposed

of the patient responsibility portion of their hospital charges, which 
may be reduced by amounts reasonably related to assets considered pur-
suant to subsection (3) of this section.
(2) At a minimum, a hospital not subject to subsection (1) of 
this section shall grant charity care per the following guidelines:
(a) All patients and their guarantors whose income is not more

(b) All patients and their guarantors whose income is between 201

(3)(a) If a hospital considers the existence, availability, and 
value of assets in order to reduce the discount extended, it must es-
tablish and make publicly available, as described in WAC 246-453-015, 
a policy on asset considerations and corresponding discount reduc-
tions.
(b) If a hospital considers assets, the following types of assets 
shall be excluded from consideration:
(i) The first $5,000 of monetary assets for an individual or 
$8,000 of monetary assets for a family of two, and $1,500 of monetary 
assets for each additional family member. The value of any asset that 
has a penalty for early withdrawal shall be the value of the asset af-
ter the penalty has been paid;
(ii) Any equity in a primary residence;
(iii) Retirement plans other than 401 (k) plans;
(iv) One motor vehicle and a second motor vehicle if it is neces-
sary for employment or medical purposes;
(v) Any prepaid burial contract or burial plot; and
(vi) Any life insurance policy with a face value of $10,000 or 
less.
(c) In considering assets, a hospital may not impose procedures 
which place an unreasonable burden on the responsible party. Informa-
tion requests from the hospital to the responsible party for the veri-
fication of assets shall be limited to that which is reasonably neces-
sary and readily available to substantiate the responsible party's 
qualification for charity sponsorship and may not be used to discour-
age application for such sponsorship. Only those facts relevant to el-
igibility may be verified and duplicate forms of verification may not 
be demanded.
(i) In considering monetary assets, one current account statement 
shall be considered sufficient for a hospital to verify a patient's 
assets.
(ii) In the event that no documentation for an asset is availa-
ble, a hospital shall rely upon a written and signed statement from 
the responsible party.

(d) Asset information obtained by the hospital in evaluating a 
patient for charity care eligibility shall not be used for collection

WAC 246-453-050   ((Guidelines for the development of sliding fee

(1) In developing these sliding fee schedules, hospitals shall

(a) The sliding fee schedule shall consider the level of charges 
that are not covered by any public or private sponsorship in relation

of charges for which the responsible party will be expected to provide

the specific financial situation of the responsible party;
(c) The sliding fee schedule shall take into account the poten-

to provide payment over a reasonable period of time, without interest 
or late fees; and

may be considered by appropriate hospital personnel for purposes of 
adjusting the amount resulting from the application of the sliding fee 
schedule, such as:
(i) Extraordinary nondiscretionary expenses relative to the 
amount of the responsible party's medical care expenses;
(ii) The existence and availability of family assets, which may 
only be considered with regard to the applicability of the sliding fee 
schedule;
(iii) The responsible party's future income earning capacity, es-
pecially where his or her ability to work in the future may be limited 
as a result of illness; and
(iv) The responsible party's ability to make payments over an ex-
tended period of time.

ered by public or private sponsorship limited to forty percent of the 
excess of that person's annual family income over one hundred percent 
of the federal poverty standard, adjusted for family size. This re-

WAC 246-453-060   Denial of access to emergency care based upon 
ability to pay and transfer of patients with emergency medical condi-
tions or active labor.   (1) No hospital or its medical staff shall 
adopt or maintain admission practices or policies which result in:
(a) A significant reduction in the proportion of patients who 
have no third-party coverage and who are unable to pay for hospital 
services;
(b) A significant reduction in the proportion of individuals ad-
mitted for inpatient hospital services for which payment is, or is 
likely to be, less than the anticipated charges for or costs of such 
services; or
(c) The refusal to admit patients who would be expected to re-
quire unusually costly or prolonged treatment for reasons other than 
those related to the appropriateness of the care available at the hos-
pital.
(2) No hospital shall adopt or maintain practices or policies 
which would deny access to emergency care based on ability to pay. No 
hospital which maintains an emergency department shall transfer a pa-
Washington State Register, Issue 26-16 WSR 26-16-047
Certified on 8/13/2026 [ 37 ] WSR Issue 26-16 - Proposed

tient with an emergency medical condition or who is in active labor 
unless the transfer is performed at the request of the patient or is 
due to the limited medical resources of the transferring hospital. 
Hospitals must make transfers to other hospitals in such circumstances 
and as promptly as dictated by the standard of care and  follow reason-
able procedures in making transfers to other hospitals including con-
firmation of acceptance of the transfer by the receiving hospital.
(3) The department shall monitor hospital compliance with subsec-
tions (1) and (2) of this section. The department shall report ((to 
the legislature and the governor on hospital compliance with these re-
quirements and shall report )) individual instances of possible noncom-
pliance to the state attorney general or the appropriate federal agen-
cy((. For purposes of monitoring compliance with subsection (2) of 
this section, the department is to follow all definitions and require-
ments of federal law )).

section, nothing in this section shall be interpreted to indicate that 
hospitals and their medical staff are required to provide ((appropri-
ate hospital-based medical services )) medically necessary hospital 
health care , including experimental services, to any individual.
(5) For the purpose of this section:
(a) "Active labor" means a stage of labor during which a person 
is having contractions and:
(i) There is inadequate time to effect a safe transfer to another 
hospital before delivery; or
(ii) Transfer may pose a threat to the health or safety of the 
pregnant person or their embryo or fetus.
(b) "Limited medical resources" means the nonavailability of 
services or medical expertise which are required or are expected to be 
required for the appropriate diagnosis, treatment, or stabilization 
per federal requirements of an individual's medical or mental situa-
tion;
(c) "Medical staff" means health care providers delivering or 
overseeing the delivery of health care services in a hospital.
(d) "Unusually costly or prolonged treatment" means those serv-
ices or combinations of services the cost of which is two standard de-
viations or more above the average charge or the duration of which is 
three standard deviations or more above the average length of stay, as 
determined by the department's discharge database.

Purpose of the Proposal and Its Anticipated Effects, Including 
Any Changes in Existing Rules: The department is seeking to update 
hunting rules to improve their enforceability and to ensure that they 
are better understood by the public.
Reasons Supporting Proposal: The department is seeking certain 
amendments to rules to improve their enforceability. By implementing 
these rule changes, the department will improve the clarity and under-
standing of these regulations to gain greater public compliance with 
the hunting laws. Improved compliance will ultimately yield better 
outcomes for preserving wildlife resources and improved public safety.

77.04.055, and 77.12.047.

decision.

WSR 26-16-057
PROPOSED RULES
PUGET SOUND
CLEAN AIR AGENCY
[Filed July 31, 2026, 2:41 p.m.]
Original Notice.
Proposal is exempt from the preproposal statement of inquiry un-
der RCW 70A.15.2040.
Title of Rule and Other Identifying Information: Amend Puget 
Sound Clean Air Agency (PSCAA) Regulation I, Section 6.03, pertaining 
to exemptions from notice of construction permitting for new or modi-
fied sources of air pollution.
Section 6.03 (b) Notifications:
Section 6.03 (b)(3) is amended to remove the permitting exemption 
for relocation of portable asphalt plants.
Section 6.03 (b)(6) is amended to remove the permitting exemption 
for wastewater evaporators.
Section 6.03 (b)(11) is amended to change the permitting exemp-
tion threshold for coffee roasters from 10 pounds per batch to 5 kilo-
grams (kg) per batch.
Section 6.03 (c) Exemptions:
Section 6.03 (c)(19)(A) is amended to clarify that thermal cut-
ting of stainless steel installed on or before November 1, 2013, is 
exempt from permitting.
Section 6.03 (c)(21) is amended to add citric acid, carbonic 
acid, and methanesulfonic acid to the list of exempt acids and to add 
potassium hydroxide to the list of exempt oxidizing agents.
Section 6.03 (c)(29) is amended to add an exemption from permit-
ting for filament-based three-dimensional printing, for resin-based 
three-dimensional printing where resin usage is less than 7,500 kg per 
year, and for laser sintering of nylon or plastic powders where powder 
usage is less than 7,500 kg per year, along with associated post-pro-
cessing breakout.
Section 6.03 (c)(39) is amended to add an exemption for direct 
laser marking, etching, or engraving equipment controlled by high-ef-
ficiency particulate air (HEPA) filtration.
Section 6.03 (c)(51)(B) is amended to add air-cooled condensers 
to the types of condensers allowed in an exempt closed-loop solvent 
recovery system.
Section 6.03 (c)(53) is amended to clarify the various subsec-
tions are joined by the conjunction "or."
Section 6.03 (c)(78)(B) is amended to add asphalt storage and 
transfer terminals to the list of facility types at which storage of 
asphalt is not exempt from permitting.
Section 6.03 (c)(102) is amended to clarify the exemption for 
small bakery ovens for yeasted bread products.
Section 6.03 (c)(106) is amended to add cannabis to the list of 
materials for which grinding, blending, packaging, or storing is ex-
empt from permitting, and to remove an inadvertent duplicated inclu-
sion of spices in this list.
Section 6.03 (c)(108) is amended to add a permitting exemption 
for fermentation tanks for the production of distilled beverages, and 
for storage or aging of distilled alcoholic beverages if no more than 
75,000 gallons of distilled alcoholic beverages are stored or aged in 
wood barrels.
Washington State Register, Issue 26-16 WSR 26-16-057
Certified on 8/13/2026 [ 43 ] WSR Issue 26-16 - Proposed

Section 6.03 (c)(109) is amended to add a permitting exemption 
for the storage of wine if no more than 360,000 gallons of wine is 
stored or aged in wood barrels.
Section 6.03 (c)(132) is amended to clarify that the permitting 
exemption for nonproduction bench-scale research equipment is intended 
to apply to testing on a small spatial scale, such as on a laboratory 
bench or laboratory worktable or inside a laboratory hood.
Hearing Location (s): On September 24, 2026, at 8:45 a.m., via 
Zoom at https://us02web.zoom.us/j/87677729331, Webinar ID 876 7772 
9331; or Call in 833-548-0282 US toll free; or in person at PSCAA, 
1904 3rd Avenue, Suite 105, Seattle, WA.
Date of Intended Adoption: October 22, 2026.
Submit Written Comments to: John Dawson, PSCAA, 1904 3rd Avenue, 
Suite 105, Seattle, WA 98101, email RegUpdates@pscleanair.gov, fax 
206-343-7522, beginning August 17, 2026, 8:00 a.m., by September 25, 
2026, 4:30 p.m.
Assistance for Persons with Disabilities: Contact agency recep-
tionist, phone 206-343-8800, fax 206-343-7522, email 
RegUpdates@pscleanair.gov, by September 21, 2026.
Purpose of the Proposal and Its Anticipated Effects, Including 
Any Changes in Existing Rules: Amend PSCAA Regulation I, Section 6.03, 
pertaining to exemptions from notice of construction permitting for 
new or modified sources of air pollution. These updates adjust the ex-
emptions from permitting, based on new technologies and new source 
categories and on questions and feedback received from businesses and 
from the public.
New or modified stationary sources of air pollution within the 
four-county PSCAA jurisdiction are required to go through the notice 
of construction permitting process, unless they fall under one of ex-
emptions from permitting in PSCAA's regulations. These categorical ex-
emptions from permitting are intended to minimize the regulatory bur-
den on source categories that PSCAA has determined have minimal impact 
on air quality and to focus PSCAA resources on source categories with 
more impact on air quality.
PSCAA's Regulation I, Section 6.03 includes approximately 149 
items in its list of notice of construction permitting. PSCAA is pro-
posing adjustment of 15 of these items in the exemptions list.
Reasons Supporting Proposal: These exemptions from permitting are 
intended to minimize the regulatory burden on source categories that 
PSCAA has determined have minimal impact on air quality and to focus 
PSCAA resources on source categories with more impact on air quality. 
PSCAA's reasoning behind each change is described as follows.
Section 6.03 (b) Notifications:
Section 6.03 (b)(3) is amended to remove the permitting exemption 
for relocation of portable asphalt plants. While in some areas of the 
country portable asphalt plants frequently relocate with large paving 
jobs, this appears to not be the case within PSCAA's four-county ju-
risdiction. In practice, asphalt plants that may be designed to be 
portable tend to stay in one location for many years. As a result, the 
major assumption behind allowing "portable" asphalt batch plants to 
relocate without new source review and permitting is not reasonably 
applicable within PSCAA's four-county jurisdiction. Additionally, as-
phalt plants are frequently cited by the public in complaints regard-
ing odors. As a result, PSCAA has determined that including asphalt 
plants in this permitting exemption for portable sources is no longer 
Washington State Register, Issue 26-16 WSR 26-16-057
Certified on 8/13/2026 [ 44 ] WSR Issue 26-16 - Proposed

appropriate, and any impacts and regulatory requirements associated 
with a relocation should be addressed through full permitting review.
Section 6.03 (b)(6) is amended to remove the permitting exemption 
for wastewater evaporators. The current exemption for wastewater evap-
orators for which the owner is able to obtain a letter from the local 
sewer district documenting that the water would otherwise meet all 
discharge limits has in practice become very difficult to use. In 
practice, local sewer districts do not generally issue such letters. 
The presence of this exemption has essentially led to a false expecta-
tion that an evaporator might not need to go through permitting when, 
in fact, the required letter from the sewer district has become nearly 
impossible to obtain. As such, PSCAA proposes removing this exemption, 
which would result in all new or modified wastewater evaporators need-
ing to go through the notice of construction permitting process.
Section 6.03 (b)(11) is amended to change the permitting exemp-
tion threshold for coffee roasters from 10 pounds per batch to 5 kg 
per batch. Most coffee roaster manufacturers size their roasters in 
kilograms, rather than pounds, since they are generally global, often 
European, businesses. PSCAA has decided to use these more common units 
in this exemption. Five kilograms is equal to 11 pounds, which would 
constitute a trivial increase in the size of roaster that can use this 
exemption.
Section 6.03 (c) Exemptions:
Section 6.03 (c)(19)(A) is amended to clarify that thermal cut-
ting of stainless steel installed on or before November 1, 2013, is 
exempt from permitting. This is a simple clarification of an existing 
exemption, rather than an actual change in the exemption criteria.
Section 6.03 (c)(21) is amended to add citric acid, carbonic 
acid, and methanesulfonic acid to the list of exempt acids. This ex-
emption is related to surface preparation of metals, which often re-
lies on the use of acids. The exemption aims to exclude certain lower-
emitting and/or low-toxicity acids from requiring permitting. In re-
cent years, PSCAA has issued case-by-case exemptions for acids that 
fit these criteria, but were not listed in this exemption. PSCAA has 
determined that these additional acids are appropriate to add to this 
exemption, because the use of these acids would have a minimal impact 
on air quality, consistent with the acids that are already included in 
this exemption. Additionally, while the use of potassium hydroxide is 
already covered under "alkaline oxidizing agents," PSCAA is adding it 
explicitly to the list of exempt materials for the sake of clarity.
Section 6.03 (c)(29) is amended to add an exemption from permit-
ting for filament-based three-dimensional printing, for resin-based 
three-dimensional printing where resin usage is less than 7,500 kg per 
year, and laser sintering of nylon or plastic powders where powder us-
age is less than 7,500 kg per year, along with associated post-pro-
cessing breakout. Three-dimensional printing is not addressed in 
PSCAA's permitting exemptions, which would imply that 3D printing is 
not currently exempt from permitting. However, PSCAA is unaware of any 
submitted applications for a permit from a 3D printer operator. The 
existing exemption for extrusion equipment could potentially be inter-
preted to include filament 3D printing, since this is essentially an 
extrusion process, in which plastic filament is melted and essentially 
squeezed through a nozzle to form the desired shape. Filament 3D 
printing is associated with emissions of small particles in the imme-
diate vicinity of the printer, but has minimal associated volatile or-
ganic compound (VOC) emissions.
Washington State Register, Issue 26-16 WSR 26-16-057
Certified on 8/13/2026 [ 45 ] WSR Issue 26-16 - Proposed

For resin-based 3D printing, there are often organic liquids in-
volved that can result in emissions of VOCs and toxic air pollutants. 
Using the common example of a resin that contains styrene, and using 
the department of ecology's de minimis emissions rate for styrene of 
3.2 pounds per day, along with the assumption that five percent of 
resin is volatilized off as styrene emissions, we calculate the fol-
lowing:
(3.2 lb styrene per day) × (260 workdays per year)/(0.05 lb styr-
ene/lb resin) = 16,640 lb resin per year.
Resin is generally sold by the kilogram, so this is converted to 
7,500 kg resin per year.
The styrene/VOC emissions factor is likely much lower than the 
five percent assumed for this analysis.
Similar logic was extended to the use of laser sintering of plas-
tic powders. If a one percent emission rate is assumed, the use of 
7,500 kg of powder yields approximately 165 lb/yr of VOC, which is a 
very small emission rate.
Small users such as individuals, schools, and makerspaces would 
be expected to remain well below this threshold. PSCAA believes that 
only large industrial users would potentially cross this resin thresh-
old, in which case those 3D printing operations would be subject to 
regular notice of construction permitting.
Section 6.03 (c)(39) is amended to add an exemption for direct 
laser marking, etching, or engraving equipment controlled by HEPA fil-
tration. Laser marking, etching, and engraving entail a superficial 
contact with the surface to be marked. This is in contrast to full la-
ser cutting, which penetrates through the entire substance to be cut. 
As such, the emissions associated with marking, etching, and engraving 
are smaller than those associated with cutting. The best available 
control technology determinations for emissions associated with laser 
marking, etching, or engraving have generally included the use of HEPA 
filtration. This new exemption would only apply if HEPA filtration is 
used. Otherwise, the operation would be subject to full notice of con-
struction permitting.
Section 6.03 (c)(51)(B) is amended to add air-cooled condensers 
to the types of condensers allowed in an exempt closed-loop solvent 
recovery system. This exemption is for closed-loop solvent recovery 
with condensers. Air-cooled condensers provide an option for condens-
ing solvent vapors, thus preventing emissions.
Section 6.03 (c)(53) is amended to clarify the various subsec-
tions are joined by the conjunction "or." This is a simple clarifica-
tion of an existing exemption, rather than an actual change in the ex-
emption criteria.
Section 6.03 (c)(78)(B) is amended to add asphalt storage and 
transfer terminals to the list of facility types at which storage of 
asphalt is not exempt from permitting. In this exemption, asphalt 
tanks at sources other than the listed sources are exempt from permit-
ting. This is meant to keep small tanks from requiring permits. One 
larger source that was not included in this list was asphalt storage 
and transfer terminals, which can have many tanks and can be a signif-
icant source of VOCs and odors. By adding this source type to this 
list, new or modified asphalt tanks at asphalt storage and transfer 
terminals would no longer be exempt from permitting and would go 
through normal notice of construction permitting. (Note that PSCAA is 
aware of just one such facility within its jurisdiction.)
Washington State Register, Issue 26-16 WSR 26-16-057
Certified on 8/13/2026 [ 46 ] WSR Issue 26-16 - Proposed

Section 6.03 (c)(102) is amended to clarify the exemption for 
small bakery ovens for yeasted bread products. This is a simple clari-
fication of an existing exemption, rather than an actual change in the 
exemption criteria.
Section 6.03 (c)(106) is amended to add cannabis to the list of 
materials for which grinding, blending, packaging, or storing is ex-
empt from permitting, and to remove an inadvertent duplicated inclu-
sion of spices in this list. PSCAA has recently become aware of a 
handful of businesses that simply repackage cannabis from larger con-
tainers to smaller ones. This type of activity alone does not cause 
air emissions that would require notice of construction review. This 
type of business is very similar to the other types listed in this ex-
emption. Additionally, the storage of cannabis at, for example, a re-
tail store is not an operation PSCAA has subject to permitting, and 
yet there is not currently a permitting exemption under which these 
types of operations would clearly fall. This clarifies that simple re-
packaging and storage of cannabis are not subject to permitting; while 
production, processing, and extraction will continue to be required to 
be permitted.
Section 6.03 (c)(108) is amended to add a permitting exemption 
for fermentation tanks for the production of distilled beverages, and 
for storage or aging of distilled alcoholic beverages if no more than 
75,000 gallons of distilled alcoholic beverages are stored or aged in 
wood barrels. PSCAA's permitting exemptions do not currently list any 
operations related to distilled spirits, even though there are exemp-
tions related to beer making and wine making. The wine exemption is 
for all fermentation to make wine, and the beer exemption is for all 
operations at facilities that produce less than three million gallons 
per year of beer.
This change to the exemptions will extend similar logic to dis-
tilled spirits. The actual fermentation is exempt at all wineries and 
almost all breweries. As such, it is logical to extend this fermenta-
tion exemption to distilleries. This exemption would cover the fermen-
tation operation at distilleries, from which emissions are minimal, 
and the barrel aging of distilled beverages if no more than 75,000 
gallons is stored, assuring that only operations that cause minimal 
emissions would be exempt from notice of construction permitting.
Much of the VOC emission from distilleries comes from the aging 
of product in wood barrels. This is usually in the form of evaporative 
losses of ethanol. In the Environmental Protection Agency's AP-42, 
Section 9.12.3, the evaporative emissions rate of 6.9 pounds per bar-
rel per year was reported. Using a standard barrel size of 53 gallons, 
the storage of 75,000 gallons of distilled beverages yields approxi-
mately five tons per year of VOC (ethanol) emissions. Ethanol emis-
sions from distilleries can be associated with "whiskey fungus" in the 
nearby community.
Note that distilleries are not currently exempt from permitting 
in PSCAA's jurisdiction. However, PSCAA has not received a permit ap-
plication from a distillery, and it has issued one case-by-case exemp-
tion from permitting for a distillery that does not age product on-
site.
Section 6.03 (c)(109) is amended to add a permitting exemption 
for the storage of wine if no more than 360,000 gallons of wine is 
stored or aged in wood barrels. PSCAA's permitting exemptions current-
ly list fermentation tanks at wineries, but they do not include other 
operations at wineries. Presumably, then, aging of wine would be cur-
rently subject to permitting. However, PSCAA has not received an ap-
Washington State Register, Issue 26-16 WSR 26-16-057
Certified on 8/13/2026 [ 47 ] WSR Issue 26-16 - Proposed

plication for a permit for winemaking in its jurisdiction. PSCAA staff 
believe wineries may be misinterpreting this exemption to include op-
erations other than just fermentation.
Based on its exemption for most breweries, and its proposed ex-
emption for fermentation at distilleries, PSCAA plans to maintain the 
exemption for fermentation at wineries. However, wine aging in barrels 
can be a significant source of VOC (ethanol) emissions. After an ex-
tensive literature review, Santa Barbara Air Pollution Control Dis-
trict determined an appropriate evaporative emissions factor for red 
wine aging in oak barrels to be 0.0278 pounds of VOC (ethanol) per 
gallon per year. Using this emissions factor, five tons per year of 
VOC emissions would result from the storage of 360,000 gallons of wine 
in wood barrels. For this reason, PSCAA set the exemption threshold at 
360,000 gallons of wine stored in wood barrels. This threshold of five 
tons per year of VOC emissions is the same as the proposed threshold 
for distilleries. Storage of wine at facilities that store 360,000 
gallons or more of wine in barrels would then require notice of con-
struction review.
Section 6.03 (c)(132) is amended to clarify that the permitting 
exemption for nonproduction bench-scale research equipment is intended 
to apply to testing on a small spatial scale, such as on a laboratory 
bench or laboratory worktable or inside a laboratory hood. PSCAA staff 
receive many questions about the applicability of this exemption for 
laboratory testing and nonproduction bench-scale research equipment. 
Some sources have interpreted this to include any sort of prototype 
development, which is not PSCAA's intent. PSCAA hopes to emphasize the 
small spatial scale on which this exemption is intended to apply. 
PSCAA's definition of "bench-scale" was inspired by the metals engi-
neering company Creative Engineers, Inc., to describe their small-
scale research and development work.
Statutory Authority for Adoption: Chapter 70A.15 RCW.
Statute Being Implemented: Chapter 70A.15 RCW.
Rule is not necessitated by federal law, federal or state court 
decision.
Name of Proponent: PSCAA, governmental.
Name of Agency Personnel Responsible for Drafting: John Dawson, 
1904 3rd Avenue, Suite 105, Seattle, WA 98101, 206-689-4060; Implemen-
tation and Enforcement: Steve Van Slyke, 1904 3rd Avenue, Suite 105, 
Seattle, WA 98101, 206-689-4052.
A school district fiscal impact statement is not required under 
RCW 28A.305.135.
A cost-benefit analysis is not required under RCW 34.05.328.  RCW 
34.05.328 does not apply to local air agencies, per RCW 70A.15.2040.

requirements of the Regulatory Fairness Act because the proposal:

apply to local air agencies.

WSR 26-16-061
PROPOSED RULES
WASHINGTON SAVES
GOVERNING BOARD
[Filed August 3, 2026, 12:25 p.m.]
Original Notice.
Preproposal statement of inquiry was filed as WSR 26-09-040.
Title of Rule and Other Identifying Information: Chapter 520-01 
WAC, Washington saves.
Hearing Location (s): On September 22, 2026, at 2:00 p.m., via 
Zoom. Login information to access the hearing will be posted on the 
www.wasaves.wa.gov/rulemaking website.
Date of Intended Adoption: September 23, 2026.
Submit Written Comments to: Jill Vallely, P.O. Box 41200, Olym-
pia, WA 98504-1200, email jill.vallely@dfi.wa.gov, beginning July 28, 
2026, by September 21, 2026.
Assistance for Persons with Disabilities: Contact Keera Earskine, 
phone 360-902-8760, TTY 1-800-833-6384, email 
keera.earskine@dfi.wa.gov, by September 21, 2026.
Purpose of the Proposal and Its Anticipated Effects, Including 
Any Changes in Existing Rules: The Washington saves governing board 
(board) is proposing rules to implement Washington saves, chapter 
19.05 RCW. Washington saves is a retirement program that will provide 
voluntary automatic payroll contributions to an individual retirement 
account (IRA) for persons who do not have a workplace retirement plan.
Reasons Supporting Proposal: Washington saves is a new retirement 
program that will launch by July 1, 2027. Chapter 19.05 RCW provides 
that the board may adopt rules to interpret, implement, and govern the 
Washington saves retirement program. The proposed rules will support 
the successful development and operation of the Washington saves re-
tirement program. The proposed rules define the administrative duties 
of covered employers, establish the registration and enrollment pro-
cesses, establish the employee alternative election process, set the 
default contribution rate, set the default escalation rate, establish 
a process by which persons who are not covered employees may partici-
pate in the program, and adopt other rules necessary to implement the 
program. The proposed rules will inform employers how to comply with 
the requirements of chapter 19.05 RCW, and will provide useful infor-
mation regarding the program to individual participants and members of 
the public.
Statutory Authority for Adoption: Chapter 19.05 RCW.
Statute Being Implemented: Chapter 19.05 RCW.
Rule is not necessitated by federal law, federal or state court 
decision.
Name of Proponent: Washington saves governing board, governmen-
tal.
Name of Agency Personnel Responsible for Drafting, Implementa-
tion, and Enforcement: Jonathan Herrera, 150 Israel Road S.W., Tumwa-
ter, WA 98501, 360-725-7814.
A school district fiscal impact statement is not required under 
RCW 28A.305.135.
A cost-benefit analysis is not required under RCW 34.05.328.  The 
board is not one of the entities listed in RCW 34.05.328 (5)(a)(i).
This rule proposal, or portions of the proposal, is exempt from 
requirements of the Regulatory Fairness Act because the proposal: 
Washington State Register, Issue 26-16 WSR 26-16-061
Certified on 8/13/2026 [ 49 ] WSR Issue 26-16 - Proposed

Is exempt under RCW 19.85.025 (3) as the rule content is explicit-
ly and specifically dictated by statute; and rules adopt, 
amend, or repeal a procedure, practice, or requirement re-
lating to agency hearings; or a filing or related process 
requirement for applying to an agency for a license or per-
mit.
Is exempt under RCW 19.85.025 (4).
Explanation of exemptions: Each of the rules in the proposed 
rules chapter is exempt under RCW 34.05.310 (4)(e) because the rules 
are dictated by the Washington saves statute, chapter 19.05 RCW. The 
statute at RCW 19.05.040 (13) enables the board to adopt rules to im-
plement the statute, including specific rules to establish registra-
tion and enrollment procedures, to establish opt out and alternative 
election procedures, to set the default contribution rate and default 
escalation rate, to create outreach and educational initiatives, and 
to establish a process for individuals to participate in the program. 
In addition, RCW 19.05.030 (1)-(3) and 19.05.050 (1) instruct the board 
to adopt rules establishing registration procedures and other provi-
sions for the program. The following table describes how each rule in 
the chapter is dictated by statute:
Section of Proposed Rules Dictated by Statute
WAC 520-01-010 Definitions This section creates definitions used in the rule chapter for the administration 
of the program pursuant to RCW 19.05.040(13).
WAC 520-01-020 Determination of 
Covered Employer Status
This section interprets who is a "covered employer" under RCW 19.05.010(6) 
and incorporates language from RCW 19.05.030 (3)(a)(ii)(B). It also reflects 
the definition of "employment" under RCW 19.05.010(10).
WAC 520-01-030 Covered Employer 
Registration Process and Timeline
This section sets forth the registration process for covered employers 
consistent with RCW 19.05.030 (1)(b) and (3)(a)(i). The staggered approach is 
consistent with RCW 19.05.040 (12)(e).
WAC 520-01-040 Employer Exemption This section states that the board may create a process to enable employers to 
claim an exemption by certifying that they are not covered employers. This is 
consistent with RCW 19.05.040 (12)(a). (The board must "ensure … that any 
employer that is not a covered employer shall have no reporting or registration 
obligation or requirement to take any action under the program other than to 
claim an exemption from coverage by the program.")
WAC 520-01-050 Employer 
Administrative Duties
This section defines covered employer administrative duties as required by 
RCW 19.05.030 (3)(a), 19.05.030 (3)(b), 19.05.040 (12)(a), and 19.05.090(1).
WAC 520-01-055 Employer Restrictions 
and Prohibitions
This section defines covered employer administrative duties as required by 
RCW 19.05.030 (3)(a) and (3)(b).
WAC 520-01-060 Program Informational 
Materials
This section specifies the content of the program materials required to be 
developed by the board under RCW 19.05.040(14).
WAC 520-01-070 Covered Employee 
Enrollment or Right to Opt Out
This section describes the covered employee enrollment or opt-out procedure 
consistent with RCW 19.05.030 (1)(a) and (c), 19.05.030 (2)(a), and 
19.05.040 (13)(a) and (b).
WAC 520-01-080 Noncovered Employee 
Participant Enrollment Procedure
This section describes the process by which noncovered employees may 
participate in the program, consistent with RCW 19.05.040 (13)(e).
WAC 520-01-090 Contributions, Default 
Contribution Rate, Automatic Escalation, 
Default Investment Options, Alternative 
Elections, Non-Payroll Contributions, 
Withdrawals, and Portability
This section describes the employee's responsibilities regarding contributions 
consistent with RCW 19.05.040 (14)(b)(viii); sets default contribution rate 
consistent with RCW 19.05.030 (1)(d) and (2)(a); sets a default automatic 
escalation rate consistent with RCW 19.05.030 (1)(e); sets a default 
investment choice consistent with RCW 19.05.030 (2)(b); describes a 
procedure for alternative elections consistent with RCW19.05.030 (2)(a) and 
(b); describes a process for nonpayroll contributions consistent with RCW 
19.05.030 (2)(c) and 19.05.040 (13)(e); describes the ability to withdraw, 
rollover, or transfer funds consistent with RCW 19.05.030 (2)(d); and 
describes the portability of accounts consistent with RCW 19.05.030 (2)(c).
WAC 520-01-100 Abandoned Accounts This section addresses abandoned accounts and is reasonable to adopt under 
RCW 19.05.040(13) for the administration of the program.

Section of Proposed Rules Dictated by Statute
WAC 520-01-110 Program Limitations This section states the program limitations consistent with RCW 19.05.040 
(14) and (18), 19.05.030 (3)(c), 19.05.050 (1)(b), and 19.05.010(8).
WAC 520-01-120 Termination of 
Covered Employer Status
This section creates a procedure that applies when a covered employer adopts 
a qualified retirement plan and thus no longer meets the definition of a 
covered employer under RCW 19.05.010. This is reasonable to adopt under 
RCW 19.05.040(13) for the administration of the program.

Employer Administrative Duties
This section interprets the enforcement provisions in RCW 19.05.030(3),

Amounts Due
This section interprets the enforcement provisions in RCW

This section creates rules regarding privacy and confidentiality consistent with 
RCW 19.05.100 (4) and (5)(d).

and Education
This section summarizes the outreach, marketing, and education provisions 
under RCW 19.05.020(3), 19.05.040(10), 19.[05.]040 (12)(d), and 19.05.040 
(13)(d).
In addition, certain of the proposed rules apply solely to indi-
vidual participants who will open IRAs under the Washington saves pro-
gram. Therefore, certain of the proposed rules are exempt under RCW 
19.05.025(4) because they do not affect small businesses as defined in 
RCW 19.85.020 (3). The following proposed rules will not affect small 
businesses: WAC 520-01-070, 520-01-080, 520-01-090, and 520-01-100.
RCW 34.05.310 (4)(g)(ii) provides an exemption from the require-
ment to prepare an economic impact statement for "rules that adopt, 
amend, or repeal a filing or related process requirement for applying 
to an agency for a license or permit." This exemption is applicable to 
the proposed rule at WAC 520-01-030, which describes the registration 
process for covered employers.
Finally, the proposed rule chapter implements the provisions of 
the Washington saves statute as set forth in chapter 19.05 RCW, and 
does not impose additional costs on businesses as compared to the 
statute. The rules inform impacted businesses how to comply with the 
administrative duties imposed on them by the statute to register with 
the program, facilitate payroll deduction, and distribute program in-
formational materials. The proposed rules chapter does not itself im-
pose more-than-minor costs on businesses, so an economic impact state-
ment is not required under RCW 19.85.030 (1).
Scope of exemption for rule proposal:
Is fully exempt.

WASHINGTON SAVES
NEW SECTION
WAC 520-01-010   Definitions.   The following definitions apply for 
the purpose of this chapter unless the context clearly requires other-
wise.
(1) "Administrative account" means the Washington saves adminis-
trative trust account created in RCW 19.05.110.
(2) "Administrative agency" means the state agency or office that 
will provide administrative support to the governing board, beginning 
no later than July 1, 2027.
(3) "Automatic escalation" means an automatic annual increase in 
a covered employee's contributions pursuant to RCW 19.05.030 (1)(e) 
and WAC 520-01-090.
(4) "Beneficiary" means the individual (s), person (s), or enti-
ty(ies) entitled to receive the proceeds of an account upon the death 
of an individual participant.
(5) "Complainant" means a covered employee, or that employee's 
designee who has written or legal authority to act on behalf of the 
employee, who files a complaint alleging an employer administrative 
violation of RCW 19.05.030 who learned of the alleged violation by way 
of their employment with a covered employer.
(6) "Contribution" means any moneys contributed to an individual 
account.
(7) "Contribution rate" means the percentage of a covered employ-
ee's wages to be withheld and contributed to covered employee's indi-
vidual account via payroll deduction under the program.
(8) "Consumer price index" means the consumer price index for all 
urban consumers, all items, for the Seattle area as calculated by the 
United States Bureau of Labor Statistics or its successor agency.
(9) "Covered employee" means an individual who is 18 years of age 
or older, who is employed by a covered employer.
(10) "Covered employer" means any employer that:
(a) Has been in business in this state for at least two years as 
of the immediately preceding calendar year;
(b) Maintains a physical presence;
(c) Does not offer a qualified retirement plan to any employees; 
and
(d) Employs, and at any point during the immediately preceding 
calendar year employed, employees working a combined minimum of 10,400 
hours.
(11) "Department" means the department of labor and industries.
(12) "Electronic fund transfer" has the same meaning as the term 
established under 15 U.S.C. 1693a (7).
(13) "Employee" means any person who is working in employment as 
defined in RCW 50.04.100.
(14) "Employer" means a person or entity engaged in a business, 
profession, trade, or other enterprise in the state, whether for prof-
it or not for profit. "Employer" does not include federal, state, or 
tribal entities, agencies, or instrumentalities, or any political sub-
division thereof.
Washington State Register, Issue 26-16 WSR 26-16-061
Certified on 8/13/2026 [ 52 ] WSR Issue 26-16 - Proposed

(15) "Employer administrative duties" include all requirements of 
covered employers under RCW 19.05.030 that do not involve amounts due 
to the employee.
(16) "Employment" has the same meaning as in RCW 50.04.100.
(17) "Governing board" means the board created in RCW 19.05.040.
(18) "Individual account" means an IRA established by or for an 
individual participant and owned by the individual participant pur-
suant to this chapter.
(19) "Individual participant" means any individual who is contri-
buting to, or has a balance credited in, an IRA through the program.
(20) "Internal revenue code" means the federal Internal Revenue 
Code of 1986, as amended, or any successor law.
(21) "Investment manager" means the person (s) selected by the 
governing board to provide investment management services and self-di-
rected investment options for the program.
(22) "IRA" means a traditional or Roth individual retirement ac-
count or individual retirement annuity described in section 408 (a), 
408(b), or 408A of the Internal Revenue Code.
(23) "Noncovered employee participant" means any person who en-
rolls in the program independent of an employment relationship with a 
covered employer, maintains an individual account, and is not a cov-
ered employee.
(24) "Nonpayroll contribution" means a contribution made by an 
individual participant to the individual participant's individual ac-
count remitted outside of payroll deduction.
(25) "Payroll deduction IRA agreement" or "payroll deduction" 
means an arrangement by which a participating employer makes payroll 
deductions authorized by this chapter and remits amounts deducted as 
contributions to IRAs on behalf of individual participants.
(26) "Program" means the Washington saves program established un-
der chapter 19.05 RCW.
(27) "Program administrator" means the third-party person (s) se-
lected by the governing board to administer the daily operations of 
the program and assist in carrying out the requirements of Washington 
Saves, chapter 19.05 RCW.
(28) "Program informational materials" means the disclosure docu-
ment provided by the program administrator to individual participants 
that describes the program features including, but not limited to, the 
items set forth in WAC 520-01-060.
(29) "Qualified retirement plan" means a retirement plan in com-
pliance with applicable federal law for employees including those de-
scribed in section 401 (a), 401 (k), 403 (a), 403 (b), 408 (k), or 408 (p) 
of the Internal Revenue Code.
(30) "Wages" means any commission, compensation, salary, or other 
remuneration, as defined by section 219 (f)(1) of the Internal Revenue 
Code, received by a covered employee from a covered employer.
NEW SECTION
WAC 520-01-020   Determination of covered employer status.   (1) An 
employer is a covered employer as defined in WAC 520-01-010 if:
(a) The employer has been in business in this state for at least 
two years as of the end of the immediately preceding calendar year;
(b) The employer currently maintains a physical presence in this 
state;
Washington State Register, Issue 26-16 WSR 26-16-061
Certified on 8/13/2026 [ 53 ] WSR Issue 26-16 - Proposed

(c) The employer does not offer a qualified retirement plan to 
any employees, or has not enabled participation of any employees in a 
qualified retirement plan offered by a trade association, chamber of 
commerce, or professional employer organization; and
(d) The employer had employed employees working at least 10,400 
combined hours in this state during the immediately preceding calendar 
year.
(2) An employer maintains a physical presence in this state if 
the employer has a substantial nexus with the state, which includes 
having employees in this state.
(3) An employee is working for an employer in this state if the 
employer is liable for paying unemployment tax for the employee in 
this state.
(4) If a professional employer organization as defined in RCW 
50.04.298(1) enters into a professional employer agreement as defined 
in RCW 50.04.298 (7) with a client employer as defined in RCW 
50.04.298(2), then for the purposes of compliance with chapter 19.05 
RCW and this chapter, the covered employer with respect to any employ-
ees defined in RCW 50.04.298 (4) is the client employer.
(5) The number of employee hours worked for an employer is deter-
mined by the hours an employer reports to the Washington employment 
services department under a single federal employer identification 
number in the employer's quarterly tax and wage reports.
NEW SECTION
WAC 520-01-030   Covered employer registration process and time-
line.  (1) An employer that is a covered employer as described in WAC 
520-01-020 as of July 1, 2027, must register with the program by the 
following dates:
(a) Covered employers who employed employees working more than 
20,800 combined hours in the state during the preceding calendar year 
must enroll in the program no later than December 1, 2028; and
(b) All other covered employers must enroll in the program no 
later than December 1, 2029.
(2) An employer that becomes a covered employer after December 1, 
2028, must register with the program no later than December 1st of the 
year in which the employer became a covered employer.
(3) To register with the program, covered employers must submit 
the following information to the program administrator through the 
program's website:
(a) Employer name, legal name, and "doing business as" name, if 
applicable;
(b) Federal employer identification number (FEIN);
(c) Employer mailing address;
(d) Employer physical address;
(e) Name, title, phone number, and email address of an individual 
designated by the employer as the primary contact for program corre-
spondence; and
(f) Any additional information reasonably required by the program 
administrator for the purpose of administering the program.
Washington State Register, Issue 26-16 WSR 26-16-061
Certified on 8/13/2026 [ 54 ] WSR Issue 26-16 - Proposed

NEW SECTION
WAC 520-01-040   Employer exemption.   (1) The program or the pro-
gram administrator may send employers a notice stating the program or 
program administrator has determined that the employer is a covered 
employer subject to registration under WAC 520-01-030.
(2) An employer contacted pursuant to subsection (1) of this sec-
tion may certify an exemption from the registration requirement in WAC 
520-010-030 by certifying through the program website that the employ-
er either presently offers a qualified retirement plan or has enabled 
employee participation in a qualified retirement plan offered by a 
trade association, chamber of commerce, or professional employer or-
ganization.
(3) After an employer submits a satisfactory and complete certif-
ication pursuant to subsection (2) of this section, the program admin-
istrator will promptly provide a confirmation of exempt status that 
will remain in effect until the employer meets the criteria of a cov-
ered employer under WAC 520-01-020.
(4) If an employer certification is found to be inaccurate or in-
complete, or the employer does not meet the program exemption stand-
ards, the program or department may pursue remedies pursuant to RCW 
19.05.070.
NEW SECTION
WAC 520-01-050   Covered employer administrative duties.   This 
section establishes the administrative duties of covered employers. To 
fulfill the requirements established for covered employers in RCW 
19.05.030(3) and this chapter, a covered employer must complete or 
otherwise comply with the following administrative duties:
(1) By or before the registration deadline specified in WAC 
520-01-030, covered employers must register with the program as de-
scribed in WAC 520-010-030 or offer a qualified retirement plan or en-
able participation of employees in a qualified retirement plan offered 
by a trade association, chamber of commerce, or professional employer 
organization.
(2) Within 30 days of registration with the program, a covered 
employer must provide the following enrollment information to the pro-
gram administrator for each covered employee:
(a) Covered employee's full legal name;
(b) Covered employee's Social Security number or individual tax-
payer identification number;
(c) Covered employee's date of birth;
(d) Covered employee's physical address;
(e) Covered employee's phone number, if available;
(f) Covered employee's email address, if available; and
(g) Any other information reasonably required by the program ad-
ministrator.
(3) For each covered employee hired by a covered employer after 
the covered employer's registration with the program, the covered em-
ployer must provide the information specified in subsection (2) of 
this section to the program administrator within 30 days of the cov-
ered employee's hire date.
(4) For each current employee who turns 18 years old and there-
fore becomes a covered employee, the covered employer must provide the 
Washington State Register, Issue 26-16 WSR 26-16-061
Certified on 8/13/2026 [ 55 ] WSR Issue 26-16 - Proposed

information specified in subsection (2) of this section to the program 
administrator no later than 30 days after the employee's 18th birth-
day.
(5) Each payroll period, a covered employer must remit the con-
tributions of each participating employee to the program administrator 
at the applicable contribution rate. The applicable contribution rate, 
and any changes a covered employee subsequently makes to the contribu-
tion rate, will be reported to the covered employer by the program ad-
ministrator through the program website or other means. A covered em-
ployer must remit all withheld contributions to the program adminis-
trator as soon as administratively practicable, generally not to ex-
ceed seven business days from the date of deduction. The covered em-
ployer must begin remitting the covered employee's contributions as 
soon as administratively practicable, but no later 30 days after noti-
fication by the program administrator of the covered employee's en-
rollment or change of contribution rate. The covered employer may not 
remit a covered employee's contributions prior to the expiration of 
the 30-day opt-out period for the covered employee.
NEW SECTION
WAC 520-01-055   Employer restrictions and prohibitions.   A cov-
ered employer, and any employer voluntarily facilitating payroll de-
duction for noncovered employee participants in the program, must not:
(1) Prohibit, restrict, or discourage covered employee participa-
tion in the program;
(2) Provide individual participants or beneficiaries of deceased 
individual participants advice or direction regarding investment 
choices, contribution rates, participation in automatic escalation, or 
any other decision about the program;
(3) Remit any contributions for any covered employee who opted 
out of the program;
(4) Exercise any authority, control, or responsibility for the 
program, other than as set forth in WAC 520-01-050;
(5) Receive consideration in the form of cash or otherwise in 
connection with the program, other than reasonable compensation for 
services actually rendered in connection with payroll deductions or 
dues checkoffs;
(6) Contribute to a covered employee's or noncovered employee 
participant's individual account; or
(7) Withhold impermissibly any amounts due to an employee related 
to the employer's obligations under RCW 19.05.030.
NEW SECTION
WAC 520-01-060   Program informational materials.   (1) The program 
administrator will provide informational materials regarding the pro-
gram to each covered employee as soon as administratively possible af-
ter the program administrator has received the covered employee's en-
rollment information from the covered employer pursuant to WAC 
520-01-050 (2) or (3).
(2) The informational materials will include instructions regard-
ing how to opt out of the program, change contribution rates, select 
Washington State Register, Issue 26-16 WSR 26-16-061
Certified on 8/13/2026 [ 56 ] WSR Issue 26-16 - Proposed

investment options, adjust or opt out of automatic escalation, make 
nonpayroll contributions, rollover or transfer funds, make withdraw-
als, establish a traditional IRA, update contact information, add or 
change a beneficiary, and make other individual account changes. The 
informational materials will also disclose all fees and other legal 
and material information that a reasonable investor would want to know 
before contributing to an individual account by payroll deduction or 
nonpayroll contribution.
(3) In addition to the information described in subsection (2) of 
this section, the informational materials will include the following:
(a) A description of the benefits and risks associated with the 
program;
(b) Instructions about how to obtain additional information about 
the program;
(c) A description of the tax consequences of an IRA, which may 
consist of or include the disclosure statement required to be distrib-
uted by the trustee under the Internal Revenue Code and treasury regu-
lations thereunder;
(d) A statement that covered employees seeking financial advice 
should contact their own financial advisers and that covered employers 
are not in a position to provide financial advice, and are not liable 
for decisions covered employees make regarding the program;
(e) A statement that the program is not an employer-sponsored re-
tirement plan;
(f) A statement that covered employee's IRA established under the 
program is not guaranteed by the state;
(g) A statement that the program is voluntary for covered employ-
ees, and a covered employee may opt out of the program at any time;
(h) A statement that neither a covered employer nor the state 
will monitor or has an obligation to monitor a covered employee's or 
noncovered employee participant's eligibility under the Internal Reve-
nue Code to make contributions to the IRA established for a covered 
employee or by a noncovered employee participant under the program or 
monitor whether a covered employee's or noncovered employee partici-
pant's contributions to the IRA established for a covered employee or 
by a noncovered employee participant under the program exceed the max-
imum permissible IRA contribution; that it is a covered employee's or 
noncovered employee participant's responsibility to monitor such mat-
ters; and that the state, the program, and the covered employer have 
no liability with respect to any failure of a covered employee or non-
covered employee participant to be eligible to make IRA contributions 
or any contribution in excess of the maximum IRA contribution;
(i) A description of the annual contribution limits that apply to 
Roth IRAs and traditional IRAs, and a description of how an individu-
al's modified adjusted gross income may limit eligibility to invest in 
a Roth IRA or lower the amount that an individual may contribute;
(j) A description of how the individual participant may commence 
the rollover or transfer process for an individual account; and
(k) A statement that withdrawals from an individual account may 
be subject to early withdrawal penalties under the Internal Revenue 
Code, and may be subject to income tax obligations.
(l) A statement that any moneys placed in individual accounts may 
not be counted as assets for the purposes of state or local means-tes-
ted program eligibility or levels of state means-tested program eligi-
bility.
