RDS-7099.5
Chapter 296 -120 WAC
PREGNANCY-RELATED ACCOMMODATION
NEW SECTION
WAC 296-120-100   Applicability.   This chapter applies to employee 
and employer conduct, acts, or omissions related to pregnancy accommo-
dation under chapter 49.92 RCW occurring on or after January 1, 2027.
Washington State Register, Issue 26-16 WSR 26-16-079
Certified on 8/13/2026 [ 147 ] WSR Issue 26-16 - Proposed

NEW SECTION
WAC 296-120-105   Definitions.   The definitions in this section 
apply throughout this chapter unless the context clearly requires oth-
erwise.
(1) "Department" means the department of labor and industries.
(2) "Director" means the director of the department of labor and 
industries or authorized representative.
(3) "Employee" means an employee who is employed in the business 
of the employee's employer whether by way of manual labor or other-
wise.
(4) "Employer" has the same meaning and must be interpreted con-
sistent with how that term is defined in RCW 49.60.040, except that 
for the purposes of this chapter only, "employer" includes any employ-
er who employs one or more persons and any religious or sectarian or-
ganization not organized for private profit.
(5) "Interim accommodation" means a temporary measure, agreed 
upon between the employee and employer to support an employee's preg-
nancy related needs while a permanent reasonable accommodation is be-
ing identified, evaluated, or implemented. An interim accommodation is 
not a substitute for, and does not replace, a permanent reasonable ac-
commodation. It is intended to allow the employee to continue working 
during this transitional period. Leave will not be considered an in-
terim accommodation unless the employee voluntarily selects or re-
quests it as such.
(6) "Pregnancy" includes the employee's pregnancy, childbirth, 
and related prenatal and postpartum mental and physical health condi-
tions, including the need to express breast milk.
(7) "Reasonable accommodation" means the following:
(a) Providing more frequent, longer, or flexible restroom breaks;
(b) Modifying a no food or drink policy;
(c) Job restructuring, part-time or modified work schedules, re-
assignment to a vacant position, or acquiring or modifying equipment, 
devices, or an employee's workstation;
(d) Providing seating or allowing the employee to sit more fre-
quently if the employee's job requires the employee to stand;
(e) Providing for a temporary transfer to a less strenuous or 
less hazardous position;
(f) Providing assistance with manual labor and limits on lifting;
(g) Scheduling flexibility for prenatal and postpartum visits, 
including allowing the use of leave required under chapter 49.12 or 
49.46 RCW;
(h) Providing reasonable break time for an employee to express 
breast milk for two years after the child's birth each time the em-
ployee has a need to express milk and providing a private location 
which may be used by the employee to express breast milk according to 
WAC 296-120-125; and
(i) Any further pregnancy accommodation an employee may request, 
and to which an employer must give reasonable consideration in consul-
tation with information provided on pregnancy accommodation by the de-
partment or the attending health care provider of the employee.
(8) "Undue hardship" means an action requiring significant diffi-
culty or expense. Undue hardship does not apply to the accommodations 
listed under WAC 296-120-110 (2).
Washington State Register, Issue 26-16 WSR 26-16-079
Certified on 8/13/2026 [ 148 ] WSR Issue 26-16 - Proposed

NEW SECTION
WAC 296-120-110   Employer responsibilities.   (1) Employers are 
required to make a reasonable accommodation or interim accommodation 
for an employee for pregnancy, unless the employer can demonstrate 
that doing so would cause undue hardship on the employer's program, 
enterprise, or business.
(2) Employers cannot claim undue hardship for the following rea-
sonable accommodations or interim accommodations:
(a) Providing more frequent, longer, or flexible restroom breaks.
(b) Modifying a no food or drink policy.
(c) Limiting lifting more than 17 pounds.
(d) Providing seating or allowing the employee to sit more fre-
quently if the employee's job requires the employee to stand.
(3) Employers may not:
(a) Deny a job or other employment opportunities to an otherwise 
qualified employee based on the employee's need for reasonable accom-
modation or interim accommodation;
(b) Require an employee to take leave if another reasonable ac-
commodation or interim accommodation can be provided for the employ-
ee's pregnancy;
(c) Retaliate against an employee for exercising any right under 
this chapter or take adverse action against an employee who requests, 
declines, or uses an accommodation under chapter 49.92 RCW that af-
fects the terms, conditions, or privileges of employment.
(4) Chapter 49.92 RCW and applicable rules do not require an em-
ployer to create additional employment that the employer would not 
otherwise have created, unless the employer does so or would do so for 
other classes of employees who need accommodation.
(5) Chapter 49.92 RCW and applicable rules do not require an em-
ployer to discharge any employee, transfer any employee with more se-
niority, or promote any employee who is not qualified to perform the 
job, unless the employer does so or would do so to accommodate other 
classes of employees who need accommodation.
NEW SECTION
WAC 296-120-115   Undue hardship —Burden of proof.   (1) The em-
ployer has the burden of demonstrating that providing reasonable ac-
commodation would impose an undue hardship on the employer's program, 
enterprise, or business.
(2) In determining whether a reasonable accommodation would im-
pose an undue hardship on an employer, factors to be considered may 
include:
(a) The nature and cost of the accommodation;
(b) The overall financial resources of the employer;
(c) The overall size of the employer's business with respect to 
the number of employees and the number, type, and location of the 
available facilities;
(d) The accommodation's effect on expenses and resources or its 
effect upon the operations of the employer; and
(e) The employer's provision of similar accommodations to other 
classes of employees.
Washington State Register, Issue 26-16 WSR 26-16-079
Certified on 8/13/2026 [ 149 ] WSR Issue 26-16 - Proposed

NEW SECTION
WAC 296-120-120   Written certification.   (1) An employer may re-
quest that the employee provide written certification from the employ-
ee's treating health care professional regarding the need for reasona-
ble accommodation, but an employer cannot request the employee provide 
written certification from the employee's treating health care profes-
sional regarding the following accommodations:
(a) Providing more frequent, longer, or flexible restroom breaks;
(b) Modifying a no food or drink policy;
(c) Providing seating or allowing the employee to sit more fre-
quently if the employee's job requires the employee to stand;
(d) Providing reasonable break time for an employee to express 
breast milk for two years after the child's birth each time the em-
ployee has a need to express milk and providing a private location 
which may be used by the employee to express breast milk; and
(e) Limiting lifting more than 17 pounds.
(2) A written certification is considered complete if it affirms 
the employee's need for reasonable accommodation or identifies reason-
able accommodation needed due to pregnancy.
(3) An employer may not request or require additional medical in-
formation or that a written certification include additional informa-
tion than in subsection (2) of this section to justify the need for 
reasonable accommodation.
(4) A written certification may be submitted using any form or 
medium. An employer may not require the use of any specific form for 
an employee to submit a written certification.
NEW SECTION
WAC 296-120-125   Protections for the expression of milk.   (1) An 
employer must provide reasonable break time for an employee to express 
breast milk for two years after the child's birth each time the em-
ployee has a need to express milk. "Reasonable break time" means a 
break period that is appropriate, adequate, or proportionate given the 
circumstances and is in addition to meal and break time required by 
chapter 49.12 RCW, WAC 296-126-092, or WAC 296-131-020. Circumstances 
that factor into the duration of a reasonable break time include, but 
are not limited to:
(a) Time needed to travel to and from the location for expressing 
milk;
(b) Time needed to travel to and from storage location for ex-
pressed milk if not within the location for expressing milk; and
(c) Time needed to clean milk expression equipment.
(2) An employer must provide a private location, other than a 
bathroom, if such a location exists at the place of business or work-
site, which may be used by the employee to express breast milk. A 
"private location" means a location that is shielded from view and 
protected from intrusion by others. The location must be clean and 
maintained in a condition appropriate for expressing milk and func-
tional for expressing milk.
(3) If a private location does not exist at the place of business 
or worksite, the employer must work with the employee to identify a 
convenient location and work schedule to accommodate their needs.
Washington State Register, Issue 26-16 WSR 26-16-079
Certified on 8/13/2026 [ 150 ] WSR Issue 26-16 - Proposed

(4) Any break time and time traveling to a location identified by 
the employer in subsections (2) and (3) of this section must be paid 
to the employee at the employee's regular compensation rate as defined 
in RCW 49.46.020. This constitutes a wage payment requirement as de-
fined in RCW 49.48.082.
(5) An employer may not require an employee to use paid leave 
during reasonable break time or travel time to express milk.
(6) An employer may not penalize or treat an employee adversely 
based on the employee's use of reasonable break time or work schedule 
changes made pursuant to this section, including if the employee's 
ability to meet quota, points, or other incentives system is impacted.
(7) An employer may not reduce or otherwise lessen an employee's 
pay based on work schedule changes made under subsection (3) of this 
section.
NEW SECTION
WAC 296-120-130   Reasonable accommodation enforcement —Com-
plaints, investigations, and citations.   (1) Upon receiving a com-
plaint alleging one or more violations of reasonable accommodations 
requirements under chapter 49.92 RCW or any associated rules, the de-
partment shall investigate the complaint. The department will not ac-
cept complaints alleging violations of reasonable accommodation re-
quirements that occurred more than three years before the date that 
the department received the complaint.
(2) During an investigation, if the department discovers informa-
tion suggesting additional violations of chapter 49.92 RCW, or any as-
sociated rules, the department may investigate and take appropriate 
enforcement action without any additional complaint.
(3) Prior to issuing any order under this section, the department 
must first contact the employer and attempt in good faith to reach an 
agreement on reasonable accommodation or interim accommodation.
(4) If the department and the employer are unable to reach an 
agreement, the department may issue an order of immediate restraint 
under WAC 296-120-135.
(5) Upon investigation of a complaint under this section, the de-
partment may issue either a citation and notice of assessment or a de-
termination of compliance within 90 days after the date on which the 
department received the complaint, unless the complaint is otherwise 
resolved. The department may extend the period by providing advance 
written notice to the complainant and the employer setting forth good 
cause for an extension of the period and specifying the duration of 
the extension.
(6) If the department's investigation finds that an employee's 
allegation cannot be substantiated, the department may issue a deter-
mination of compliance or closure letter to the employee and the em-
ployer detailing such findings.
(7) If the department's investigation finds that the employer 
violated the accommodation requirements of chapter 49.92 RCW or asso-
ciated rules, and the complaint is not otherwise resolved, the depart-
ment may issue a citation and notice of assessment.
(8) The department's notice of assessment, pursuant to RCW 
49.48.083, may order the employer to:
(a) Provide reasonable accommodation as required;
Washington State Register, Issue 26-16 WSR 26-16-079
Certified on 8/13/2026 [ 151 ] WSR Issue 26-16 - Proposed

(b) Cease using any policy that is not in compliance with the re-
quirements of chapter 49.92 RCW or associated rules;
(c) Modify any policy that interferes with an employee's rights 
under chapter 49.92 RCW or associated rules;
(d) Adopt policies that ensure the employer's compliance with, 
and notify employees of their rights under, chapter 49.92 RCW and any 
associated rules;
(e) Provide employees, including managers and human resources 
personnel, training approved by the department on the requirements of 
chapter 49.92 RCW or associated rules;
(f) Pay all amounts owed due to the employer's failure or refusal 
to make reasonable accommodation, including interest of one percent 
per month on all amounts owed, to the employee;
(g) Provide the employee any combination of reinstatement and 
payment of leave hours; and
(h) Pay the department a civil penalty as specified in WAC 
296-120-140.
(9) The department shall send a citation and notice of assessment 
or a determination of compliance to both the employer and the complai-
nant by service of process or using a method by which the mailing can 
be tracked, or the delivery can be confirmed to their last known ad-
dresses.
(10) The department may continue to monitor compliance throughout 
the duration of reasonable accommodation. The department may initiate 
a new investigation, issue additional temporary orders as defined un-
der WAC 296-120-135, or issue additional determinations, without the 
filing of an additional complaint.
NEW SECTION
WAC 296-120-135   Order of immediate restraint.   (1) If, during 
the investigation of a complaint under WAC 296-120-130, the department 
and the employer are unable to reach an agreement, the department may 
issue a temporary order immediately restraining any such condition, 
practice, method, process, or means in the workplace that violates any 
provision of chapter 49.92 RCW or associated rules.
(2) The order of immediate restraint may not be in effect longer 
than 90 calendar days. To extend the order beyond 90 calendar days, 
the department must seek a restraining order, or other such relief as 
appears appropriate under the circumstances, in the superior court of 
the county wherein such conditions of employment or practice exist.
(3) An employer may contest an order restraining any condition of 
employment or practice issued under subsection (1) of this section 
within 10 working days of the effective date of the order by making an 
application to the superior court of the county wherein such condi-
tions of employment or practice exist. Upon the filing of any such pe-
tition, the superior courts of the state of Washington shall have ju-
risdiction to grant appropriate relief from the order.
(4) Any employer who has been issued an order immediately re-
straining a condition, practice, method, process, or means in the 
workplace, pursuant to chapter 49.92 RCW or associated rules, and who 
nevertheless continues such condition, practice, method, process, or 
means, the department may issue a civil penalty per WAC 296-120-140 
for each day an employer continues such condition, practice, method, 
process, or means that is in violation.

Certified on 8/13/2026 [ 152 ] WSR Issue 26-16 - Proposed

NEW SECTION
WAC 296-120-140   Reasonable accommodation enforcement —Civil pen-
alties.  (1) The department may assess civil penalties for violations 
of reasonable accommodation requirements under chapter 49.92 RCW or 
associated rules. For violations of reasonable accommodation require-
ments related to expression of milk, the department may assess a civil 
penalty under this chapter or RCW 49.17.530 but may not assess dupli-
cative penalties for the same violation. For the purposes of a civil 
penalty under this section, each occurrence affecting an individual 
employee seeking reasonable accommodation constitutes a separate vio-
lation.
(2) For employers with fewer than 15 employees, civil penalties 
for first time violations will be not less than $1,500 for each viola-
tion. For employers with 15 or more employees, civil penalties for 
first time violations will be not less than $3,000 for each violation. 
In addition to the base minimum penalty, the department may increase 
the penalty amount considering the totality of the circumstances in-
cluding, but not limited to, consideration of following factors:
(a) The employer size and resources;
(b) The gravity of the violation, including the nature, extent, 
the potential harm of the violations, and the amounts owed the worker 
due to the violations;
(c) The good faith of the employer, including the promptness of 
responding to requests for reasonable or interim accommodation, ef-
forts to work in consultation with the employee to identify and make 
reasonable or interim accommodation, actions to avoid unnecessary de-
lays, and cooperation with the department's investigation; and
(d) The employer's history of previous complaints and violations 
of pregnancy-related accommodations under chapter 49.92 RCW and the 
associated rules, including settlements.
(3) The assessed penalty under subsection (2) of this section is 
increased for repeat violations as follows:
(a) 1st time × 2.5
(b) 2nd time × 5
(c) 3rd time × 10
(4) The department may, at any time, waive or reduce any civil 
penalty assessed against an employer under this section if the depart-
ment determines that the employer has taken corrective action.
(5) The department must deposit all civil penalties paid under 
this section in the supplemental pension fund established under RCW 
51.44.033.
NEW SECTION
WAC 296-120-145   Reasonable accommodation enforcement —Appeals. 
(1) A person, firm, or corporation aggrieved by a citation and notice 
of assessment or a determination of compliance issued by the depart-
ment under WAC 296-120-130 may appeal the citation and notice of as-
sessment by filing a notice of appeal with the department within 30 
days of the department's issuance of the citation and notice of as-
sessment. A citation and notice of assessment or a determination of 
compliance not appealed within 30 days is final and binding, and not 
subject to further appeal.
Washington State Register, Issue 26-16 WSR 26-16-079
Certified on 8/13/2026 [ 153 ] WSR Issue 26-16 - Proposed

(2) A notice of appeal filed with the department under this sec-
tion shall stay the effectiveness of the citation and notice of as-
sessment or a determination of compliance pending final review of the 
appeal by the department as provided for under chapter 34.05 RCW.
(3) Upon receipt of a notice of appeal, the department shall as-
sign the hearing to an administrative law judge of the office of ad-
ministrative hearings to conduct the hearing and issue an initial or-
der. The hearing and review procedures shall be conducted in accord-
ance with chapter 34.05 RCW, and the standard of review by the admin-
istrative law judge of an appealed citation and notice of assessment 
or determination of compliance shall be de novo. Any party who seeks 
to challenge an initial order shall file a petition for administrative 
review with the department within 30 days after service of the initial 
order. The department shall conduct administrative reviews in accord-
ance with chapter 34.05 RCW.
(4) The department shall issue all final orders after appeal of 
the initial order. The final order of the department is subject to ju-
dicial review in accordance with chapter 34.05 RCW.
(5) Orders that are not appealed within the time period specified 
in this section and chapter 34.05 RCW are final and binding, and not 
subject to further appeal.
(6) An employer who fails to allow adequate inspection of records 
in an investigation by the department under this section within a rea-
sonable time period may not use such records in any appeal under this 
section to challenge the correctness of any verification by the de-
partment of the penalty assessed.
NEW SECTION
WAC 296-120-150   Retaliation.   (1) It is unlawful for an employer 
to interfere with, restrain, or deny the exercise of any employee 
right provided under or in connection with chapter 49.92 RCW or asso-
ciated rules.
(2) It is unlawful for an employer to take any adverse action 
against an employee because the employee has exercised their rights 
provided under chapter 49.92 RCW or associated rules. Such rights in-
clude, but are not limited to: Requesting, declining, or using an ac-
commodation; filing an action, or instituting or causing to be insti-
tuted any proceeding under or related to chapter 49.92 RCW or associ-
ated rules; exercising any right to pregnancy-related accommodations 
provided under chapter 49.92 RCW or associated rules; or cooperating 
as a witness including testifying or intending to testify in any such 
investigation or proceeding related to any rights provided under chap-
ter 49.92 RCW or associated rules.
(3) Adverse action means any action taken or threatened by an em-
ployer against an employee for their exercise of chapter 49.92 RCW, or 
rights under associated rules which may include, but are not limited 
to:
(a) Terminating, suspending, demoting, or denying a promotion;
(b) Withholding opportunities such as professional development 
and leadership engagements that would otherwise be available;
(c) Reducing or changing the number of work hours for which the 
employee is scheduled;
(d) Altering the employee's preexisting work schedule;
(e) Reducing the employee's rate of pay;
Washington State Register, Issue 26-16 WSR 26-16-079
Certified on 8/13/2026 [ 154 ] WSR Issue 26-16 - Proposed

(f) Otherwise impact the terms or conditions of employment;
(g) Threatening to take, or taking action, based on the immigra-
tion status of the employee or an employee's family member.
NEW SECTION
WAC 296-120-155   Retaliation enforcement —Complaints, investiga-
tions, and citations.   (1) An employee who believes that they were 
subject to retaliation by their employer, as defined in WAC 
296-120-150, for the exercise of any employee right under chapter 
49.92 RCW or associated rules, may file a complaint with the depart-
ment. The department will investigate complaints filed within 180 days 
of the alleged retaliatory action. The department may, at its discre-
tion, extend the 180-day period based on recognized equitable princi-
ples or because extenuating circumstances exist. For example, the de-
partment may extend the 180-day period when there is evidence that the 
employer has concealed or misled the employee regarding the alleged 
retaliatory action.
(2) If an employee files a timely complaint with the department 
or the department initiates an investigation alleging retaliation, the 
department will investigate the complaint and issue either a citation 
and notice of assessment or a determination of compliance within 90 
days after the date on which the department received the complaint, 
unless the complaint is otherwise resolved. The department may extend 
the period by providing advance written notice to the employee and the 
employer setting forth good cause for an extension of the period and 
specifying the duration of the extension.
(3) The department may consider a complaint to be otherwise re-
solved when the employee and the employer reach a mutual agreement to 
remedy any retaliatory action, or the employee voluntarily and on the 
employee's own initiative withdraws the complaint. Mutual agreements 
include, but are not limited to, rehiring, reinstatement, back pay, 
and reestablishment of benefits.
(4) If the department's investigation finds that the employee's 
allegation of retaliation cannot be substantiated, the department will 
issue a determination of compliance to the employee and the employer 
detailing such findings.
(5) If the department's investigation finds that the employer re-
taliated against the employee, and the complaint is not otherwise re-
solved, then the department may issue a citation and notice of assess-
ment. The department's citation and notice of assessment may:
(a) Order the employer to make payable to the employee amounts 
owed due to the employer's retaliatory action, including interest of 
one percent per month on all earnings owed;
(b) Order the employer to restore the employee to the position of 
employment held by the employee when the retaliation occurred or re-
store the employee to an equivalent position with equivalent employ-
ment hours, work schedule, benefits, pay, and other terms and condi-
tions of employment;
(c) Order the employer to cease using any policy that is not in 
compliance with the requirements of chapter 49.92 RCW or associated 
rules; and
(d) Order the employer to pay the department a civil penalty as 
specified under WAC 296-120-140.
Washington State Register, Issue 26-16 WSR 26-16-079
Certified on 8/13/2026 [ 155 ] WSR Issue 26-16 - Proposed

(6) The department will send the citation and notice of assess-
ment or determination of compliance to both the employer and employee 
by service of process or using a method by which the mailing can be 
tracked or the delivery can be confirmed to their last known address-
es.
(7) During an investigation of the employee's retaliation com-
plaint, if the department discovers information suggesting alleged vi-
olations by the employer of the employee's other rights under chapter 
49.92 RCW or associated rules, the department may investigate and take 
appropriate enforcement action without requiring the employee to file 
a new or separate complaint. If the department determines that the em-
ployer violated additional rights of the employee under chapter 49.92 
RCW or associated rules, the employer may be subject to additional en-
forcement actions for the violation of such rights. If the department 
discovers information alleging the employer retaliated against or oth-
erwise violated rights of other employees under chapter 49.92 RCW or 
associated rules, the department may launch further investigation un-
der chapter 49.92 RCW or associated rules, without requiring addition-
al complaints to be filed.
NEW SECTION
WAC 296-120-160   Retaliation enforcement —Civil penalties.   (1) 
If the department's investigation finds that an employer retaliated 
against an employee, pursuant to the procedures outlined in WAC 
296-120-155, the department may order the employer to pay the depart-
ment a civil penalty as specified under WAC 296-120-140.
(2) The department may, at any time, waive or reduce any civil 
penalty assessed against an employer under this section if the depart-
ment determines that the employer has taken corrective action to reme-
dy the retaliatory action.
(3) The department will deposit civil penalties paid under this 
section in the supplemental pension fund established under RCW 
51.44.033.
NEW SECTION
WAC 296-120-165   Retaliation enforcement —Appeals.  (1) A person, 
firm, or corporation aggrieved by a citation and notice of assessment 
or a determination of compliance may, within 30 days after the date of 
such decision, submit a request for reconsideration to the department 
setting forth the grounds for seeking such reconsideration, or submit 
an appeal to the department pursuant to the procedures outlined in 
subsection (4) of this section. If the department receives a timely 
request for reconsideration, the department will either accept the re-
quest or treat the request as a notice of appeal. The provisions of 
this section shall not apply to an order of immediate restraint.
(2) If a request for reconsideration is accepted, the department 
will send notice of the request for reconsideration to the employer 
and the employee. The department will determine if there are any valid 
reasons to reverse or modify the department's original decision to is-
sue a citation and notice of assessment or determination of compliance 
Washington State Register, Issue 26-16 WSR 26-16-079
Certified on 8/13/2026 [ 156 ] WSR Issue 26-16 - Proposed

within 30 days of receipt of such request. The department may extend 
this period by providing advance written notice to the employee and 
employer, setting forth good cause for an extension of the period, and 
specifying the duration of the extension. After reviewing the recon-
sideration, the department will either:
(a) Notify the employee and the employer that the citation and 
notice of assessment or determination of compliance is affirmed; or
(b) Notify the employee and the employer that the citation and 
notice of assessment or determination of compliance has been reversed 
or modified.
(3) A request for reconsideration submitted to the department 
shall stay the effectiveness of the citation and notice of assessment 
or the determination of compliance pending the reconsideration deci-
sion by the department.
(4) Within 30 days after the date the department issues a cita-
tion and notice of assessment or a determination of compliance, or 
within 30 days after the date the department issues its decision on 
the request for reconsideration, a person, firm, or corporation ag-
grieved by a citation and notice of assessment or a determination of 
compliance may file with the department a notice of appeal.
(5) A notice of appeal filed with the department under this sec-
tion shall stay the effectiveness of the citation and notice of as-
sessment or the determination of compliance pending final review of 
the appeal by the department as provided for in chapter 34.05 RCW.
(6) Upon receipt of a notice of appeal, the department shall as-
sign the hearing to an administrative law judge of the office of ad-
ministrative hearings to conduct the hearing and issue an initial or-
der. The hearing and review procedures shall be conducted in accord-
ance with chapter 34.05 RCW, and the standard of review by the admin-
istrative law judge of an appealed citation and notice of assessment 
or determination of compliance shall be de novo. Any party who seeks 
to challenge an initial order shall file a petition for administrative 
review with the department within 30 days after service of the initial 
order. The department shall conduct administrative review in accord-
ance with chapter 34.05 RCW.
(7) If a request for reconsideration is not submitted to the de-
partment within 30 days after the date of the original citation and 
notice of assessment or determination of compliance, and a person, 
firm, or corporation aggrieved by a citation and notice of assessment 
or determination of compliance did not submit an appeal to the depart-
ment, then the citation and notice of assessment or determination of 
compliance is final and binding, and not subject to further appeal.
(8) The department shall issue all final orders after appeal of 
the initial order. The final order of the department is subject to ju-
dicial review in accordance with chapter 34.05 RCW.
(9) Director's orders that are not appealed within the time peri-
od specified in this section and chapter 34.05 RCW are final and bind-
ing, and not subject to further appeal.
(10) An employer who fails to allow adequate inspection of re-
cords in an investigation by the department under WAC 296-120-105 
through 296-120-170 within a reasonable time period may not use such 
records in any appeal under such rules to challenge the correctness of 
any determination by the department.

NEW SECTION
WAC 296-120-170   Enforcement —Collections.  Collections of 
amounts owed for unpaid citations and notices of assessment under this 
chapter will be handled pursuant to the procedures outlined in RCW 
49.48.086.
NEW SECTION
WAC 296-120-175   Additional rights.   (1) Nothing in this chapter 
precludes a person from pursuing a civil cause of action as provided 
under chapter 49.92 RCW.
(2) Nothing in this chapter precludes a person whose complaint is 
resolved by the department from seeking remedies provided under RCW 
49.92.030(2), including costs of suit and reasonable attorneys' fees, 
that were not addressed through the department's enforcement.
(3) Nothing in this chapter preempts, limits, diminishes, or oth-
erwise affects any other provision of law relating to sex discrimina-
tion or pregnancy, or in any way diminishes or limit legal protections 
or coverage for pregnancy, childbirth, or pregnancy-related health 
conditions.
NEW SECTION
WAC 296-120-180   Severability.   If any provision of the rules in 
this chapter, or their application to any person or circumstance is 
held invalid, the remainder of these rules or their application of the 
provision to other persons or circumstances is not affected.

WSR 26-16-083
PROPOSED RULES
DEPARTMENT OF HEALTH
(Board of Nursing)
[Filed August 5, 2026, 8:41 a.m.]
Original Notice.
Preproposal statement of inquiry was filed as WSR 26-01-020.
Title of Rule and Other Identifying Information: Nursing educa-
tion programs rules; amending WAC 246-840-500 through 246-840-571.
The Washington state board of nursing (board) is proposing amend-
ments to WAC 246-840-500 through 246-840-571 to update nursing educa-
tion program requirements, approval, faculty qualifications, curricu-
lum, clinical education, student competency, and board oversight. The 
proposed rules also implement SHB 2339 (chapter 139, Laws of 2026). 
This rule making is phase 3 of four phases of the board's review of 
chapter 246-840 WAC, conducted in accordance with RCW 43.70.041, which 
requires the board to formally review its rules at least every five 
years.
Hearing Location (s): On September 11, 2026, 1:15 p.m., at the De-
partment of Labor and Industries (L&I), 7273 Linderson Way S.W., Tum-
water, WA 98501, Room S117/118; or virtual. Please follow this link to 
register for the virtual hearing which will give you instructions to 
either join the meeting on a device, or to call in to the meeting on 
the phone, https://us02web.zoom.us/meeting/register/j6U-
yvRBQECcNnrwRbDM9w. After registering you will receive a confirmation 
email containing information.
Date of Intended Adoption: September 11, 2026.
Submit Written Comments to: Jessilyn Dagum, P.O. Box 47864, Olym-
pia, WA 98504-7864, email WABONRules@doh.wa.gov, fax 360-236-4738, web 
https://fortress.wa.gov/doh/policyreview, beginning the date and time 
of this filing, by August 21, 2026, at 11:59 p.m.
Assistance for Persons with Disabilities: Jessilyn Dagum, phone 
360-236-3538, fax 360-236-4738, TTY 711, email WABONRules@doh.wa.gov, 
by August 21, 2026.
Purpose of the Proposal and Its Anticipated Effects, Including 
Any Changes in Existing Rules: The board is proposing amendments to 
nursing education program rules in WAC 246-840-500 through 
246-840-571. The proposal updates, clarifies, and reorganizes existing 
rules for nursing programs.
The proposed rules are intended to reduce administrative burden 
for nursing programs; remove barriers related to clinical placements 
and faculty oversight; modernize simulation requirements; and simplify 
processes for program approval, expansion, and innovative program de-
livery. The proposal also aligns board requirements with national ac-
creditation standards where appropriate.
The proposed amendments:
• Update the minimum requirements for simulation in nursing educa-
tion, nursing faculty with changes to clinical experiences, and 
degree qualifications;
• Streamline chapter 246-840 WAC with national best practices and 
standards;
• Reduce administrative burden for nursing education programs while 
maintaining minimum standards set by the board;
• Remove barriers related to clinical placements and clinical fac-
ulty oversight;
Washington State Register, Issue 26-16 WSR 26-16-083
Certified on 8/13/2026 [ 159 ] WSR Issue 26-16 - Proposed

• Simplify processes for program approval, expansion, and implemen-
tation of innovative delivery methods; and
• Align board requirements, where appropriate, with national ac-
creditation standards.
The anticipated effect of the proposal is to make the rules 
clearer and easier to use while maintaining minimum standards for 
nursing education, program accountability, and public safety.
Reasons Supporting Proposal: The current nursing education pro-
gram rules have become outdated, duplicative, and unnecessarily com-
plex. The proposed amendments are needed to improve clarity, consis-
tency, and usability for nursing programs, students, faculty, and the 
public.
This rule making is part of the board's review of chapter 246-840 
WAC under RCW 43.70.041. RCW 43.70.041 requires the board to formally 
review its rules at least once every five years to simplify, stream-
line, and improve regulations. This proposal is Phase 3 of the board's 
comprehensive review of chapter 246-840 WAC. The proposed rules sup-
port current nursing education practices, reduce unnecessary adminis-
trative burden, and help nursing programs prepare graduates for safe 
and competent nursing practice. The proposed amendments also align 
with recent legislative changes related to advanced practice licensure 
and applicant documentation requirements, as described in SHB 2339 
(chapter 139, Laws of 2026). SHB 2339 makes title and terminology up-
dates for advanced practice nursing in chapter 18.79 RCW, including 
updates to advanced practice role titles (SHB 2339, sections 1-4). The 
proposed amendments align the advanced practice nursing education 
rules with the statutory terminology changes. SHB 2339 also removes 
the statutory requirement that licensure applicants submit official 
transcripts to the board and retains the requirement that applicants 
submit an attested written application on a department of health form 
and any other official records specified by the board (SHB 2339, sec-
tions 5 and 6). The board is addressing transcript-related rule 
changes across multiple rule makings. The proposed amendments in this 
rule making clarify the education verification process by requiring 
the postsecondary educational institution to submit verification that 
the applicant for licensure has met the nursing program and graduation 
requirements to facilitate timely licensing (WAC 246-840-513, section 
2).
The board anticipates the proposed rules will improve clarity and 
consistency for applicants, licensees, employers, educators, and the 
public. The proposed amendments are not anticipated to create signifi-
cant implementation or enforcement issues. The proposed amendments 
support the board's statutory authority and responsibility to regulate 
nursing practice, protect public health and safety, and conduct peri-
odic rule review.
Statutory Authority for Adoption: RCW 18.79.010 and 18.79.110.
Statute Being Implemented: RCW 18.79.110, 18.79.800, 43.70.041, 
and SHB 2339 (chapter 139, Laws of 2026). 
Rule is not necessitated by federal law, federal or state court 
decision.

This rule proposal, or portions of the proposal, is exempt from 
requirements of the Regulatory Fairness Act because the proposal: 
Is exempt under RCW 19.85.025 (3) as the rules only correct typo-
graphical errors, make address or name changes, or clarify 
language of a rule without changing its effect; and rules 
adopt, amend, or repeal a procedure, practice, or require-
ment relating to agency hearings; or a filing or related 
process requirement for applying to an agency for a license 
or permit.
Explanation of exemptions: Portions of the proposed rule are ex-
empt under RCW 34.05.310 (4)(d) because they clarify language, update 
terminology, reorganize existing requirements, and make the rules eas-
ier to read without changing the effect of those regulations and RCW 
34.05.310 (4)(g) because they update the appeal process to the board 
for program approval status, are procedural in nature, without estab-
lishing new requirements.
Scope of exemption for rule proposal:
Is partially exempt:
Explanation of partial exemptions: The proposed rule 
changes to WAC 246-840-500, 246-840-505, 246-840-514, 
246-840-521, 246-840-522, 246-840-544, 246-840-546, 
246-840-559, 246-840-561, 246-840-562, 246-840-564, 
246-840-566, 246-840-567, 246-840-568, and 246-840-569 
are exempt under RCW 34.05.310 (4)(d) because they 
clarify, reorganize, modernize, or correct language 
without changing the effect of the rule. Portions of 
the proposed changes to WAC 246-840-562 are also exempt 
under RCW 34.05.310 (4)(g) because they are procedural 
in nature and only relate to a process requirement for 
licensure. Other portions of the proposal establish or 
amend substantive nursing program requirements, includ-
ing program approval, program standards and evaluation, 
reporting and recordkeeping, faculty requirements, 
clinical and simulation education, program changes, and 
board oversight. Those portions are therefore not ex-
empt.
The proposed rule does not impose more-than-minor costs on busi-
nesses. Following is a summary of the agency's analysis showing how 
costs were calculated. The board analyzed portions of the proposed 
rule that impact small businesses and estimated probable costs that 
are not significant under RCW 34.05.310 impacting small businesses. 
The board determined that the proposed rule does not impose more-than-
minor costs on small businesses. The North American Industry Classifi-
cation System (NAICS) was used to identify the codes, 611210 and 
611310, as the best representations of the education field for nursing 
programs. The estimated costs to implement the proposed changes in 
rules totals are $0.00 to negligible costs. The proposed rule is an-
ticipated to result in no costs or only negligible costs associated 
with limited staff time to review curriculum language, simulation pol-
Washington State Register, Issue 26-16 WSR 26-16-083
Certified on 8/13/2026 [ 161 ] WSR Issue 26-16 - Proposed

icies, clinical hour calculations, or internal program documents, de-
pendent on the time in hours of labor for nursing program administra-
tors to learn the updated processes. These negligible costs are less 
than the one percent minor cost threshold of $1,576.37 and $11,015.09 
for the industry identified under NAICS 611210 and 611310, respective-
ly. The proposed amendments to update nursing programs reduce adminis-
trative burden and clarify accepted standards that are already in 
practice. Based on this analysis, the board determined that the pro-
posed rule does not impose more-than-minor costs on small businesses.

AMENDATORY SECTION  (Amending WSR 16-17-082, filed 8/17/16, effective 
9/17/16)
WAC 246-840-500   Philosophy governing approval of nursing ((edu-
cation)) programs.   ((The commission believes that quality nursing ed-
ucation provides the foundation for safe and effective nursing prac-
tice. Nursing education shall be accessible and promote student and 
faculty diversity. While the commission has established minimum stand-
ards for approved nursing education programs, it believes that each 
nursing education program should have flexibility in developing and 
implementing its philosophy, purposes, and objectives. Such develop-
ment and implementation should be based not only upon the minimum 
standards for approved nursing education programs, but also upon sound 
educational and professional principles for the preparation of regis-
tered nurses, practical nurses, advanced registered nurse practition-
ers, and other nurses who pursue graduate nursing degrees and post-
graduate degrees and certifications to meet current and future nursing 
needs of the public. The commission believes that there must be con-
gruence between the total program activities of the nursing education 
program and its stated philosophy, purpose and objectives.
The commission further believes that the standards for approved 
nursing education programs are useful for promoting self-evaluation 
and peer evaluation, which may lead to further program development and 
ongoing continuous quality improvement. )) Quality nursing education 
provides the foundation for safe and effective nursing practice. The 
board establishes the minimum standards for approval of nursing pro-
grams, and each nursing program shall have autonomy in developing and 
implementing its philosophy, purposes, and objectives. The board pro-
motes a just culture that supports learning, accountability, open com-
munication, and continuous improvement in support of safe educational 
and clinical environments.
These standards for approved nursing programs are guided by edu-
cational and professional principles for the preparation of practical 
Washington State Register, Issue 26-16 WSR 26-16-083
Certified on 8/13/2026 [ 162 ] WSR Issue 26-16 - Proposed

nurses, registered nurses, advanced registered nurse practitioners, 
and nurses pursuing additional graduate education to meet evolving 
nursing workforce needs.
National accreditation standards support quality nursing educa-
tion within nursing programs by promoting self-evaluation and peer re-
view, encouraging ongoing program development, and continuous quality 
improvement. The board recognizes these efforts support the approval 
of nursing programs that prepare students to practice safely and com-
petently, ensuring the protection of the public.
NEW SECTION
WAC 246-840-501   Definitions.   The definitions in this section 
apply throughout WAC 246-840-500 through 246-840-571 unless the con-
text clearly requires otherwise.
(1) "Clinical practice" means a faculty-planned student learning 
experience in which nursing knowledge, skills, and clinical judgment 
are applied in a health care, community, or other practice setting. 
Clinical practice may include direct care clinical practice, indirect 
practice experiences, observational clinical experiences, and simula-
tion-based learning experiences as permitted under this chapter.
(2) "Direct care clinical practice" means a clinical practice ex-
perience involving interaction with a client in which the student par-
ticipates in the assessment, planning, implementation, or evaluation 
of nursing care. Direct care clinical practice may involve individu-
als, families, groups, communities, or populations across the life-
span.
(3) "Indirect practice experience" means a faculty-planned stu-
dent learning experience that supports nursing practice or client out-
comes but does not involve direct interaction between the student and 
a client. Indirect practice experiences may include case analysis, 
leadership or policy activities, interprofessional collaboration, de-
velopment of population-based interventions, research projects, and 
the use of client or population data for quality assurance or quality 
improvement activities.
(4) "National nursing education accrediting body" means a nursing 
education accrediting body recognized by the United States Department 
of Education (USDE) that accredits nursing programs.
(5) "Observational clinical experience" means a clinical practice 
experience in which a student observes nursing practice, health care 
delivery, professional roles, or systems of care without actively par-
ticipating in direct client care.
(6) "Simulation-based learning experience" means a structured and 
facilitated activity that represents an actual or potential clinical 
situation and allows a student to develop, demonstrate, or enhance 
knowledge, skills, clinical judgment, or professional attitudes in a 
simulated environment. A simulation-based learning experience includes 
preparation, prebriefing, the simulated scenario, and debriefing.

Certified on 8/13/2026 [ 163 ] WSR Issue 26-16 - Proposed

AMENDATORY SECTION  (Amending WSR 16-17-082, filed 8/17/16, effective 
9/17/16)
WAC 246-840-505   Purposes of ((commission)) board approval of 
nursing ((education)) programs.   The ((commission)) board approves 
nursing ((education)) programs to:
(1) Assure preparation for the safe and effective practice of 
nursing by setting minimum standards for nursing ((education)) pro-
grams ((preparing persons for licensure as registered nurses, practi-
cal nurses, advanced registered nurse practitioners, or for preparing 
nurses for additional graduate education or higher levels of nursing 
practice)).
(2) Provide criteria for the approval, development, evaluation, 
and improvement of new and established nursing ((education)) programs.
(3) Assure graduates of nursing ((education)) programs are educa-
tionally prepared for licensure at the appropriate level of nursing 
practice.
(4) Facilitate interstate endorsement of graduates of ((commis-
sion)) board-approved nursing ((education)) programs.
(5) Assure nursing education standards for out-of-state distance 
learning nursing ((education)) programs placing students in Washington 
state for clinical or other practice experiences are equivalent to in-
state nursing ((education)) programs.
(6) Assure internationally educated nurses' educational prepara-
tion is equivalent to that of in-state nursing ((education)) programs.
AMENDATORY SECTION  (Amending WSR 16-17-082, filed 8/17/16, effective 
9/17/16)
WAC 246-840-510   Approval of initial (new) in-state nursing ((ed-
ucation)) programs.   (((1) New nursing education programs must submit 
a commission approved application for approval to operate a new under-
graduate, post-licensure, or graduate nursing education program in 
Washington state.
(2) Graduate programs changing from a master's degree in nursing 
to a doctoral of nursing practice degree must submit a substantive 
change request identified in WAC 246-840-554 (3).
(3) The commission shall consider the need, size, type, and geo-
graphic location when approving a program.
Phase I: Submission of application and feasibility study
(4) A postsecondary educational institution wishing to establish 
a nursing education program or additional program in nursing shall 
submit an application and feasibility study as follows:
(a) Submit to the commission a statement of intent to establish a 
nursing education program or additional program on a form provided by 
the commission and a completed feasibility study that includes the 
following information:
(i) Studies documenting the current and future supply and demand 
needs for nurses in the area of the proposed nursing education pro-
gram;
(ii) Purposes and classification of the proposed nursing educa-
tion program;
(iii) Availability of qualified candidates for the nurse adminis-
trator and faculty positions;
Washington State Register, Issue 26-16 WSR 26-16-083
Certified on 8/13/2026 [ 164 ] WSR Issue 26-16 - Proposed

(iv) Budgeted nurse administrator and faculty positions over the 
course of five years;
(v) Source and description of adequate and acceptable clinical or 
practice facilities for the nursing education program;
(vi) Description of adequate and acceptable academic facilities 
for the nursing education program;
(vii) Potential effect on other nursing programs within a sixty 
mile radius of the proposed nursing education program location;
(viii) Evidence of financial resources adequate and acceptable 
for the planning, implementation, and continuation of the nursing edu-
cation program for the next five years;
(ix) Anticipated student population;
(x) Tentative time schedule for planning and initiating the nurs-
ing education program; and
(xi) Accreditation status of the parent institution.
(b) Respond to the commission's request (s) for additional infor-
mation.
Phase II: Nursing education program development
(5) Only after receiving commission approval for nursing educa-
tion program development, the educational institution shall:
(a) Appoint a qualified nurse administrator;
(b) Provide appropriate resources, consultants, and faculty to 
develop the proposed nursing education program; and
(c) At least three months prior to advertising and admitting stu-
dents, submit the proposed program plan including the following:
(i) Program purpose and outcomes;
(ii) Organization and administration within the educational in-
stitution and within the nursing unit or department including the 
nurse administrator, faculty, and nursing support staff;
(iii) Resources, facilities, and services for students and facul-
ty;
(iv) Policies and procedures as identified in WAC 246-840-519 
(3)(a) through (e);
(v) A plan for hiring and retaining faculty, including qualifica-
tions, responsibilities, organizational structure, and faculty/student 
ratio in classroom, clinical, and practice experiences;
(vi) Curriculum, including course descriptions, course outcomes, 
and course topical outlines;
(vii) Initial year and five-year sustaining budget;
(viii) Projected plans for the orderly expansion and ongoing 
evaluation of the program.
(d) If required by the commission, arrange a site visit to the 
campus to clarify and augment materials included in the written pro-
posed program plan. The visit may be conducted by a representative of 
the commission before a decision regarding approval is made.
Phase III: Initial approval
(6) The nursing education program may only admit students if it 
has received initial approval by the commission.
(a) The nursing education program shall submit progress reports 
as requested by the commission.
(b) Site visits shall be scheduled as deemed necessary by the 
commission during the period of initial approval. A site survey, con-
ducted by the commission, will determine whether graduates may test 
for the national council licensing examination (NCLEX) as identified 
in WAC 246-840-050 or graduate certification exams as identified in 
Washington State Register, Issue 26-16 WSR 26-16-083
Certified on 8/13/2026 [ 165 ] WSR Issue 26-16 - Proposed

WAC 246-840-302 (3)(a), (b), (c) and (d) for advanced registered nurse 
practice.
Phase IV: Full approval
(7) A self-evaluation report of compliance with the standards for 
nursing education as identified in WAC 246-840-511 through 
246-840-556, shall be submitted to the nursing commission within six 
months following graduation of the first class.
(a) The commission may conduct a site visit to determine full ap-
proval of the nursing education program.
(b) The commission will review the self-evaluation report, survey 
reports and program outcome data in order to grant or deny full appro-
val of the nursing education program under WAC 246-840-558 (1).))
(1) Phase I: Submission of application. A postsecondary educa-
tional institution seeking to establish a nursing program or addition-
al nursing program shall submit the applicable application as stated 
in subsection (6) of this section, and provide any additional informa-
tion requested by the board. The application must include:
(a) A statement of intent to establish a nursing program or addi-
tional program on a form provided by the board; and
(b) The application and supporting documentation submitted to the 
accrediting body, if applicable.
(2) Phase II: Nursing program development. After receiving board 
approval for nursing program development, the educational institution 
shall:
(a) Appoint a qualified nurse administrator;
(b) Provide appropriate resources, consultants, and faculty to 
develop the proposed nursing program; and
(c) Submit the proposed program plan on a board-approved form. 
The proposed program plan must include the following:
(i) Program purpose and outcomes;
(ii) Organization structure and administration within the educa-
tional institution and within the nursing unit or department including 
the nurse administrator, faculty, and nursing support staff;
(iii) Resources, facilities, and services for students and facul-
ty;
(iv) Curriculum, including course descriptions, course outcomes, 
and course topical outlines; and
(v) Projected plans for the expansion and ongoing evaluation of 
the program.
(d) If required by the board, the program may schedule a site 
visit of the campus to clarify and augment materials included in the 
proposed written program plan.
(3) Phase III: Initial approval. After receiving initial approval 
by the board:
(a) The nursing education program may admit students;
(b) The nursing program shall submit progress reports as reques-
ted by the board; and
(c) The board will schedule site visits as necessary during the 
period of initial approval. The board will determine whether graduates 
may test for the National Council Licensing Examination (NCLEX) as 
identified in WAC 246-840-050 or graduate certification exams as de-
scribed in WAC 246-840-302 for advanced registered nurse practice.
(4) Phase IV: Full approval. The board may grant full approval 
after:
Washington State Register, Issue 26-16 WSR 26-16-083
Certified on 8/13/2026 [ 166 ] WSR Issue 26-16 - Proposed

(a) Ensuring the nursing program complies with the standards for 
nursing education identified in WAC 246-840-511 through 246-840-556 
within six months following graduation of the first class; and
(b) Reviewing the self-evaluation report, survey reports, and 
program outcome data to grant or deny full approval under WAC 
246-840-558.
(5) The board may conduct a site visit to determine full approval 
of the nursing education program.
(6) Application requirements.
(a) RN to BSN nursing programs. Nursing programs seeking board 
approval of an RN-BSN program shall apply for approval using one of 
the following streamlined pathways at the program's discretion:
(i) Submit a board-approved application with all required docu-
mentation demonstrating compliance with state nursing education regu-
lations; or
(ii) Submit a completed application and supporting materials pre-
viously prepared for a recognized national nursing accrediting body; 
or
(iii) Submit a completed application and materials prepared for a 
Washington state recognized higher education body that demonstrates 
compliance with the nursing education standards in this chapter.
(b) Graduate programs. Graduate programs changing from a master's 
degree in nursing to a doctor of nursing practice degree shall submit 
a substantive change request as required in WAC 246-840-554 (3).
(c) New nursing programs shall submit a board-approved applica-
tion for approval to operate a new undergraduate, post-licensure, or 
graduate nursing program in Washington state using a phased approach.
(d) The board shall notify programs of its decision within 30 
days.

WAC 246-840-512   Standards and evaluation of nursing ((educa-
tion)) programs.   (1) The nursing ((education)) program shall meet 
minimum standards established by the ((commission)) board as detailed 
in WAC 246-840-511 through 246-840-556. All information communicated 
by the nursing program to the board must be accurate, complete, and 
consistent with information available to students and the public 
across all platforms.
(2) The nursing ((education program shall implement a written, 
comprehensive, systematic plan for ongoing evaluation that is based on 
program outcomes data and input from faculty, students, health care 
partners and consumers, and that incorporates continuing improvement 
goals and measures.
(a) The plan must include evaluative criteria, methods used to 
evaluate, frequency of evaluation, assignment of responsibility, and 
measurable indicators or benchmarks of effectiveness for the nursing 
education program and instruction.
(b) The nursing education program shall document analysis of the 
data collected and actions taken as a result of use of the systematic 
program evaluation plan.
(c) Major changes in the professional nursing education program 
must be evidence-based. 
(d) The nursing education program shall review and analyze the 
evaluative methods and instruments used to measure program outcomes 
for appropriateness according to the timeline specified in the plan.
(e) The nursing education program shall evaluate didactic and 
clinical course effectiveness each time a course is taught.
(f) Implementation of the plan for systematic program evaluation 
and ongoing quality improvements must be documented in faculty or fac-
ulty-related minutes.
(g) The following items must be included in the systematic pro-
gram evaluation: Faculty, student and graduate satisfaction surveys, 
facility, resource and services surveys of faculty and students, fac-
ulty workload surveys and evaluations, national council licensing ex-
amination (NCLEX) pass rates, post licensure certification examination 
pass rates, student attrition and completion rates, employment rates 
Washington State Register, Issue 26-16 WSR 26-16-083
Certified on 8/13/2026 [ 168 ] WSR Issue 26-16 - Proposed

after graduation, employer satisfaction, and program and student 
learning outcomes.
(h) Faculty and students shall participate in program planning, 
implementation, evaluation, and continuous quality improvement.
(3) Program information communicated by the nursing education 
program must be accurate, complete, and consistent. )) program shall:
(a) Complete the board-approved annual education survey identify-
ing key quality indicators for nursing education; and
(b) Implement a written, comprehensive, systematic plan per ac-
creditation standards for ongoing evaluation that is based on the end 
of program student learning outcomes (EPSLOs) data and feedback from 
faculty, students, health care partners, consumers, and that incorpo-
rates continuing improvement goals and measures.
(3) The board will review programs based on national accrediting 
standards, state requirements not covered by accreditation standards, 
and the National Council State Boards of Nursing (NCSBN) prelicensure 
nursing program approval quality indicators.

WAC 246-840-513   Reporting and recordkeeping requirements for 
nursing ((education)) programs.   (((1) Within two business days, nurs-
ing education programs shall report to the commission, on forms provi-
ded by the commission, events involving a student or faculty member 
that the program has reason to believe resulted in patient harm, an 
unreasonable risk of patient harm, or diversion of legend drugs or 
controlled substances.
(2) The nursing education program shall keep a log of all events 
reported by a patient, family member, student, faculty or a health 
care provider resulting in patient harm, an unreasonable risk of pa-
tient harm, or allegations of diversion, and medication errors. The 
log must include:
(a) The date and nature of the event;
(b) The name of the student or faculty member involved;
(c) The name of the clinical faculty member responsible for the 
student's clinical experience;
(d) Assessment of findings and suspected causes related to the 
incident or root cause analysis;
(e) Nursing education program corrective action; and
(f) Remediation plan, if applicable.
(3) The nursing education program shall use the principles of 
just culture, fairness, and accountability in the implementation and 
use of all incident reporting logs with the intent of:
(a) Determining the cause and contributing factors of the inci-
dent;
(b) Preventing future occurrences;
(c) Facilitating student learning; and
(d) Using the results of incident assessments for on-going pro-
gram improvement. )) (1) For events that the nursing program has reason 
to believe resulted in patient harm, an unreasonable risk of patient 
harm, or allegations of diversion of legend drugs or controlled sub-
stances, and involve a student or faculty member, the nursing program 
shall:
Washington State Register, Issue 26-16 WSR 26-16-083
Certified on 8/13/2026 [ 169 ] WSR Issue 26-16 - Proposed

(a) Report the event to the board within two business days of re-
ceiving notice of the event; and
(b) Maintain a log of all events which includes:
(i) The date and nature of the event;
(ii) The name of the student or faculty member involved;
(iii) The name of the clinical faculty member responsible for the 
student's clinical experience;
(iv) Any assessment of findings and suspected causes related to 
the incident or root cause analysis;
(v) Any nursing program corrective action if applicable; and
(vi) Any student remediation plan, if applicable.
(c) The nursing program shall use the principles of just culture, 
fairness, and accountability in the evaluation of reportable inci-
dents.
(2) The postsecondary educational institution shall submit veri-
fication as required in this chapter that the applicant for licensure 
has met the nursing program and graduation requirements to facilitate 
timely licensing.

WAC 246-840-517   Nurse administrator qualification requirements 
in nursing ((education)) programs located in Washington state.   (1) 
The nursing ((education program administrator must be a professionally 
and academically qualified registered nurse with an active, unencum-
bered Washington nursing license.
(2) The nurse administrator of a practical or associate degree 
nursing education program must have a minimum of:
(a) The following educational qualifications:
(i) A bachelor of science in nursing (BSN) from a nursing educa-
tion program accredited by a national nursing education accrediting 
body recognized by the United States Department of Education and a 
graduate degree; or
(ii) A graduate degree from a nursing education program accredi-
ted by a national nursing education accrediting body recognized by the 
United States Department of Education;
(b) Preparation in education that includes teaching adults, adult 
learning theory, teaching methods, curriculum development, and curric-
ulum evaluation, or two years of teaching experience in nursing educa-
tion that demonstrates this type of preparation;
(c) Curriculum development and administration experience;
(d) Five years of experience as a registered nurse including two 
years of experience in nursing education; and
(e) Current knowledge of nursing practice at the practical nurse 
or associate degree program level as appropriate.
(3) The nurse administrator of a baccalaureate or graduate nurs-
ing education program must have:
(a) The following educational qualifications:
(i) A graduate degree in nursing. The graduate degree must be 
from a program accredited by a national nursing education accredita-
tion body recognized by the United States Department of Education; and 
Washington State Register, Issue 26-16 WSR 26-16-083
Certified on 8/13/2026 [ 171 ] WSR Issue 26-16 - Proposed

(ii) A doctoral degree in nursing, health, or a related educa-
tional field. The doctoral degree must be from a college or university 
accredited by both a regional accrediting body and a national nursing 
accrediting body recognized by the United States Department of Educa-
tion;
(b) Preparation in education that includes teaching adults, adult 
learning theory, teaching methods, curriculum development, and curric-
ulum evaluation, or two years of teaching experience in nursing educa-
tion that demonstrates this type of preparation;
(c) Preparation or experience in nursing administration or educa-
tional administration; and
(d) At least five years of experience as a registered nurse in-
cluding two years of experience in nursing education at or above the 
highest level of the nursing education program the nurse administrator 
will be administering.
The board may grant an exception to the two years of experience 
in nursing education requirement, for nurse administrators of graduate 
nursing education programs, if the program can demonstrate that the 
nurse administrator will be provided with two academic years of ongo-
ing educational mentorship by a person who meets or exceeds nurse ad-
ministrator qualifications identified in this subsection.
(4) The board may grant an exception to any or all of the re-
quirements in subsection (3)(a)(ii), (b), (c), or (d) of this section 
for nurse administrators of baccalaureate nursing education programs 
if the following conditions are met:
(a) The educational institution, parent institution, postsecon-
dary educational institution, or governing entity submits to the board 
for approval within 30 days of hire a written comprehensive plan to 
support the nurse administrator's success in the role.
(i) The plan must address the nurse administrator's areas of ex-
ception as they relate to any or all of the requirements in subsection 
(3)(a)(ii), (b), (c), or (d) of this section.
(ii) The plan must include strategies such as ongoing mentorship 
by a qualified nurse administrator who meets or exceeds the qualifica-
tions in subsection (3)(a), (b), (c), and (d) of this section, mentor-
ship at the academic institution, structured professional development, 
and additional education to address gaps in teaching methods, curricu-
lum development, and nursing administrative experience.
(iii) An individual with two or more years of experience as an 
academic nurse administrator is not required to have a comprehensive 
plan.
(b) An evaluation of both the comprehensive plan and the perform-
ance of the nurse administrator must occur each year for a two-year 
period. The evaluation should involve input from the nurse administra-
tor, mentors, nursing faculty, and administrative peers.
(c) An actionable development plan must be submitted to the board 
each year for two years, based on the data and analysis of the compre-
hensive plan and performance review of the nurse administrator. )) pro-
gram administrator of a nursing program located in Washington state

(RN) with an active, unencumbered RN license or privilege to practice 
in Washington state and meet requirements in the jurisdiction where

(2) The nurse administrator of a practical or associate degree 
nursing program must have:
(a) A baccalaureate degree in nursing from a nursing program ac-
credited by a national nursing education accreditation body recognized

(d) A current knowledge of nursing practice at or above the level

9/17/16)
WAC 246-840-518   Resources, facilities and services for approved 
nursing ((education)) programs.   (1) A nursing ((education)) program 
shall have the fiscal, human, physical, technological, clinical and 
learning resources ((adequate)) to support student and  program pro-
cesses and outcomes.
(2) ((Classrooms, laboratories, and conference rooms must be 
available and adequate in size, number, and type according to the num-
ber of students and the educational purposes for, which the rooms are 
to be used.
(3) Offices must be available and adequate in size, number, and 
type to provide faculty with opportunity for uninterrupted work and 
privacy for conferences with students. Adequate space must be provided 
for clerical staff, records, files, and other equipment.
(4) An office allowing for private consultation with students and 
faculty, and support for administrative responsibilities must be 
available to the nurse administrator.
(5) Library facilities and computer access must be provided for 
use by the faculty and students. Physical facilities, hours, and scope 
and currency of learning resources must be appropriate for the purpose 
of the program and for the number of faculty and students.
(6) The nursing education program shall conduct annual evalua-
tions of resources, facilities, and services based on input from fac-
ulty and students. The schedule and results of these evaluations must 
be available to the commission upon request.
(7))) The nursing ((education)) program shall demonstrate ade-
quate financial support for faculty, support personnel, equipment, 
technology, supplies, and services.

WAC 246-840-531   Clinical and practice experiences for students 
in approved nursing ((education)) programs.   (1) All nursing programs 
preparing students for licensure ((shall provide faculty planned clin-
ical or direct patient care experiences based on program outcomes and 
goals.
(a) The number of clinical or direct patient care experience 
hours must be:
(i) At least three hundred hours for licensed practical nursing 
education programs;
(ii) At least five hundred hours for associate degree nursing ed-
ucation programs;
(iii) At least six hundred hours for bachelors of science in 
nursing education programs;
(iv) At least five hundred hours for masters level nurse practi-
tioner nursing education programs;
(v) At least one thousand hours for doctoral of nursing practice 
nurse practitioner programs.
(b) Observation of licensed or qualified health care professio-
nals practicing a technical skill or therapy may be included in the 
calculation of student clinical hours. Observation is reserved for 
care or therapy situations, which students are not qualified to deliv-
er;
(c) Skill practice labs must not be counted towards clinical 
practice hours.
(2)(a) All postlicensure nursing education programs shall have 
faculty planned practice experiences for students based on program 
outcomes and goals. Practice experience examples include, but are not 
limited to: Indirect and direct patient care, patient or population 
teaching, population interventions, student nurse teaching or the 
teaching of nursing students, leadership and change projects, re-
search, accessing client or population data for the purpose of doing 
quality assurance or improvement projects, informatics, thesis or dis-
sertation development and defense.
(b) The number of practice hours must be equivalent to programs 
of similar type:
(i) At least one hundred hours for registered nurse to bachelor's 
degree programs; and
(ii) At least one hundred hours for graduate nursing education 
programs.
(3) Faculty shall organize clinical and practice experiences 
based on the educational preparation and skill level of the student.
(4) Faculty shall plan, oversee, and evaluate student clinical 
and practice experiences. )) and post-licensure shall provide faculty 
planned clinical experiences based on program outcomes and goals. Stu-
dents must demonstrate achievement of competencies adopted by the 
nursing program from nationally recognized professional standards and 
evaluated in accordance with the requirements of the applicable na-
tional nursing education accrediting body.
Washington State Register, Issue 26-16 WSR 26-16-083
Certified on 8/13/2026 [ 179 ] WSR Issue 26-16 - Proposed

(2) The total number of clinical hours shall align with the na-
tional clinical hour averages for comparable programs, as identified 
by the National Council of State Boards of Nursing (NCSBN).
(3) Clinical experience hours shall support competency develop-
ment and attainment of end of program student learning outcomes.
(4) In prelicensure programs, 50 percent or more of clinical ex-
periences in each clinical course must be direct care with clients.
(5) Observation experiences may be included in the calculation of 
student clinical hours.
(6) Skill practice labs may not be counted towards clinical prac-
tice hours.
(7) Faculty shall plan, oversee, and evaluate student clinical 
and practice experiences.
(8) The nursing program retains sole responsibility for securing, 
coordinating, and overseeing clinical placements, including procuring 
clinical sites and preceptors.

WAC 246-840-532   Faculty to student ratios for clinical and prac-
tice experience in nursing ((education)) programs.   When deemed safe 
by nursing faculty judgment:
(1) Practical and prelicensure registered nursing ((education)) 
programs shall have :
(a) A maximum faculty to student ratio of one faculty member to 
((ten)) 10 students in clinical settings involving direct patient 
care((,)); and
(b) One faculty member to ((fifteen)) 15 students at one time in 
practice settings that are observational, population or community edu-
cation projects,  involve student precepted experiences, or are skills 
practice labs.
(2) ((Registered nurse to bachelor nursing education programs 
shall have a maximum faculty to student ratio of one faculty member to 
fifteen students at one time in clinical and practice settings.
(3) Advanced registered nurse practitioner nursing education pro-
grams shall have a maximum faculty to student ratio of one faculty 
member to six students in clinical and practice settings.
(4))) Registered nurse to baccalaureate (BSN) and g raduate nurs-
ing ((education)) programs (not leading to licensure as an advanced 
registered nurse practitioner (ARNP)), shall have a maximum faculty to 
student ratio of one faculty member to ((fifteen)) 15 students in 
clinical and practice settings.
(((5) A lower ratio of faculty to students may be required for 
students in initial or highly complex learning situations, or when 
student or patient safety warrant. )) (3) Graduate nursing education 
programs that lead to licensure as an ARNP shall follow national ac-
creditation and professional guidelines.
